# Métis 2.0
## Product, architecture and delivery contract
### Orb-only when armed. Complete scope. Verified behavior. Test the signed artifact.

**Owner:** Tony Walteur · **Audience:** Codex implementation lead, Fable/Claude collaborator and authorized engineering reviewers  
**Specification date:** 23 September 2026 · **Revision:** 4.5 — Complete Codex + Fable delivery contract; governed Hindsight memory embedded in Métis (kit r11) · **Target product:** Métis 2.0  
**Document status:** implementation mandate and proposed acceptance criteria, not completed engineering or release certification.

> Build the actual product described here. Do not stop at a plan, a visual prototype, a dependency installation, or green source-pattern tests. Preserve working behavior and user data. Verify the real outcome of every enabled capability. Ship a signed Windows release only after its required gates pass; develop and test the native Mac product now, while holding its public distribution until its Apple requirements are satisfied.

This is the revised, self-contained Métis 2.0 delivery contract. The Markdown and PDF are editions of the same document. This revision is the single normative handoff. Package r10 consolidates r9, the owner’s Tony-only onboarding correction and the Codex/Fable collaboration contract. Section 32.6 now preserves the existing Métis scenes; §§33–34 define real implementation and onboarding closure. Earlier eight-stage replacement-wizard illustrations are superseded. The artifact-derived capabilities remain in §32. Package r8 incorporated the animated orb-only ARMED refinement in §5.11 and the source/experience contracts in §28–30; it takes precedence over static-idle and earlier idle-pill sketches. It supersedes previous wording where task dependencies, scope traceability, Intelligence classification, release qualification or evidence rules differ; it incorporates rather than replaces the earlier feature requirements. The Cloudflare-first defaults, optional-model rules, privacy and platform release gates remain in force. All visual concepts remain simulated design references, not application evidence. The current source-reconciled kit includes the animated solving reference and meeting workspace. Their original HTML is not an updated implementation or the source of this revision's requirements.


**Latest owner directives, applied throughout this edition:** keep the existing Métis onboarding; replace only the old welcome media with Tony Walteur’s authentic approved recording or the complete text-led fallback. Fold accessibility, context, agent and connection guidance into the existing scenes. The guarded Métis Reveal stays interactive. ARMED is only the animated solving orb. Codex and the actually authorized Fable/Claude environment cooperate through exact-source handoffs; a proposal, mock, zero-exit model call or two agents agreeing never certifies production behavior. No signed installer or deployed integration is included in this specification kit.

**Owner decisions now locked:** Cloudflare-hosted speech is the default for new Métis 2.0 installations on Windows and native Mac. Local speech and local generative models are separate, optional Settings capabilities, off and uninstalled by default unless a trusted explicit deployment policy selects them. Device detection recommends compatible packs; it never supplies consent to download or activate them. Speech content must not be persisted in Cloudflare services or diagnostic systems. Approved metadata-only portal accounting remains required. “No model training,” “no stored content,” and “no metadata” are different claims.

**Start at TASK-001, then use the ordered checkpoints in section 0 and the dependencies in section 21.** All **66 task IDs, 55 requirement IDs and UC-001–UC-112** are preserved. Revision 4.2 additionally binds the 12 EXP experience qualifications and 24 SRC source findings in sections 28–30; these refine existing task scope, not replace it. No renumbering is needed to reuse revision-3 tickets. Sections 25–27 add owner-request coverage, end-to-end proof scripts and an evidence contract. A task ID is a stable work-item identity, not a command to wait for every lower-numbered task. The urgent P1 repair lane and platform-scoped prerequisites are explicit. Section 21.3 preserves the older revision-2 crosswalk.

**Revision 4 preparation:** the complete revision-3 document and the two recovered original implementation briefs were read, their expressed scope was reconciled with this conversation, and selected primary Apple, Microsoft, Cloudflare and TypeSafe documentation was rechecked. Repository observations retain their explicitly dated historical status; this revision does not claim a fresh line-by-line repository audit or live production test. The original Claude artifact still did not yield readable content, and the exact separately promised refactoring skill/onboarding video remain inputs to identify. Their unseen contents are not certified as covered. No application, tenant, Dust workspace, provider, production database or release was changed. Structural validation of this document is not product validation.

**What this coverage audit corrected**

1. The previous embedded launch instruction stopped at UC-076 despite the 112-case catalog. It now names the whole catalog.
2. Signing now precedes final artifact acceptance: build → sign/timestamp → freeze hashes → test those exact bytes → publish without rebuilding.

4. Known P1 fixes get an immediate narrow repair lane, while their new-onboarding integration is tested later on the final candidate.
5. Jev/Laya business classifications, clock-driven stale/overdue detection, fleet-key fairness and CodeNotch/reference continuity are explicitly restored from the earlier briefs.
6. Every owner commitment is linked to requirements, use cases, implementation owners and proof. Remote stop, long-meeting memory, critical-fact scoring and recovery have explicit failure semantics.

## Codex quick start — the non-negotiables

1. **Cloudflare speech first:** new Windows and native Mac profiles select the qualified Cloudflare-hosted model. No employee account API key and no implicit capture consent.
2. **Local only by choice:** separate local speech and generation Settings; recommend from actual hardware plus a reviewed catalog, install after consent, self-test, then select explicitly.
3. **No stored cloud content:** qualify the exact route and disable/verify content logs, caches and storage. Keep the approved metadata ledger; never claim that no service metadata exists.
4. **Fast, not premature:** local wake, requested-only tracks, bounded buffers, stable segments, immediate cancellation and correct action postconditions; no privacy/approval shortcut.
5. **End-to-end means deployed:** identified native clients, real Cloudflare speech, real Jev/Laya decision, actual action, truthful UI, stream cleanup and reconciled portal record.
6. **Work in order:** TASK-001 through TASK-066, loading relevant sections on demand. Windows publication needs actual signing/evidence; native Mac publication stays separately gated by Apple requirements.

7. **Shared truth needs evidence:** canonical records own facts; Graphify/wiki are access-scoped projections; every material claim has provenance, uncertainty and correction handling.
8. **Dust is governed collaboration:** authenticate the actual user/service scope; allow safe typed writes, review consequential changes and verify canonical readback.
9. **Cloud skills, simple clients:** one versioned registry and server execution path; updates need no reinstall, revoked skills cannot execute, and run content does not become telemetry.
10. **Teams presence is not permission:** qualify actual events, admission, media/status rules and the lawful processing route. Meeting speech cannot authorize desktop actions.

## 0. Start here: delivery order, not another planning exercise

**r8 visual acceptance:** open `OPEN-METIS-2.html` and inspect orb-only ARMED → typed/wake expansion. Read §5.11 for the latest owner refinement. All provider, privacy, knowledge, Teams and release requirements below remain.

### 0.1 First working session

1. **Establish the real baseline (TASK-001).** Read applicable repository instructions, preserve dirty work and record source, installed clients, deployment versions, available tools and baseline failures. Identify existing work before writing a parallel implementation.
2. **Freeze the decision rules and unblock signing discovery (TASK-002 and TASK-005).** Map this contract into the PRD, verify legitimate Windows signing availability without exposing secrets, inventory Apple/Teams/Dust/cloud approvals, and agree the shared contracts. Do not stop unrelated work because an external approval is missing.
3. **Repair today's broken journey (TASK-027.A and TASK-028.A).** Reproduce #196/#197 against the correct renderer, carry only the necessary reviewed patch, and repair the right-edge input regression. Keep this patch separate from broad refactoring. Recheck the original fix ancestry and existing integration before cherry-picking anything.
4. **Open bounded parallel work (TASK-003–TASK-010).** Create the source map, measured footprint, identity/storage/Settings/skills/meeting contracts and owners. Start the native Mac foundation from its shared contracts, not after the entire Windows application has shipped.
5. **Implement one real private speech path (TASK-011–TASK-020).** Configure the authorized service, suppress content persistence, prove real Cloudflare speech and connect trusted local wake/capture. Use synthetic material until route and deployment privacy prerequisites are qualified.
6. **Deliver complete product slices (TASK-021–TASK-052).** Work in dependency order across optional components, lean onboarding, UI, Jev/Laya, deterministic actions, accounting, canonical knowledge, Dust, skills and meeting participation. Each slice has its own proof; one blocked adapter does not stop unrelated implementation.
7. **Qualify, refactor and operate (TASK-053–TASK-062).** Run the gold sets and golden journeys, refine performance, execute characterized refactoring, verify operational recovery and read back the deployed systems. Re-run affected acceptance after every behavior or artifact change.
8. **Build and sign the final candidate before final acceptance (TASK-063).** Freeze the exact Windows installer/runtime/helper and component-manifest identities. Test the signed candidate on native Windows and compatible deployed services. Native Mac has its own candidate/evidence record.
9. **Publish the same verified Windows bytes (TASK-064).** Verify download hashes and publisher again after upload; do not rebuild or re-sign the tested installer during publication. Maintain the separate Apple public-distribution hold (TASK-065).
10. **Close the scope honestly (TASK-066).** Return actual artifact links, evidence and remaining blockers. A scoped Windows release is not a claim that an unapproved Teams media route or the pending native Mac public release is complete.

### 0.2 Authority and completion

This document authorizes engineering intent, not fabricated credentials, legal approval or unrestricted production changes. Use already authorized repository/deployment workflows and ordinary reversible operations without asking permission at every step. New paid infrastructure, permission broadening, destructive migrations, external sends, public promotion and supplier/legal decisions follow the actual owner controls. Ask one precise question only when tooling and available source evidence cannot resolve a required input.

**Required scope cannot disappear behind feature flags.** A flag is for controlled rollout. Every promised feature retains an owner and VERIFIED or BLOCKED status. An owner-approved scoped release may exclude a blocked conditional platform route and must state that exclusion; it cannot claim full Métis 2.0 completion. Windows does not wait for unavailable Apple public signing, but shared security/privacy gates never become optional. Zero observed failures, a signed file or a beautiful demo is not universal assurance.

### 0.3 How to read this efficiently

Use this one document as the authoritative input. Generate a small repository index, task records and machine-readable acceptance map from it; those are execution artifacts, not additional user documents required to understand the scope. Keep the short invariants always loaded. Retrieve only the active sections, symbols, tests and source revisions. Never compress away unresolved failures, permissions, acceptance IDs, current branch ownership or evidence limitations. Do not feed all 112 use cases into every inference request.

## 1. Execution rules and product decisions

### 1.1 What success means

A newly authorized Windows user installs a small, signed core, reaches a usable application with Cloudflare speech selected, without receiving a Cloudflare account token or downloading unwanted local models, and sees honest setup progress. On an opted-in device, “Hey Métis” opens the command bar immediately after wake detection. The user's words appear above it. The mesh orb is inside the bar. Métis resolves the request, obtains any necessary approval, performs the action, verifies the target state, and returns a truthful result. A fleet administrator can trace the operation and its known consumption in the real Operator portal without reading the user's conversation content.

The native Mac implementation must support the same qualified product journeys through real macOS APIs and a native interface. Native implementation, native testing, Developer ID signing, notarization, App Store eligibility and PCC entitlement are distinct milestones. A DMG is a distribution container, not evidence that its application is native.

An authorized meeting can be attended visibly under the user's enrolled policy, summarized through the qualified path and saved to the correct knowledge audience. The user can ask Mantu Intelligence or a Dust agent about it, commit an allowed update and see that update across surfaces. A centrally published skill uses the right evidence without a desktop reinstall. These new journeys are separate release-qualified capabilities—not an assumption that any previous source file already provides them.

### 1.2 Decisions that override earlier planning where they conflict

| ID | Decision | Non-negotiable consequence |
|---|---|---|
| D01 | Windows 2.0 is the first public release lane. | Trusted signing, timestamping, installer/runtime checks and native user-journey evidence remain mandatory. Apple signing delays must not silently waive or permanently block these Windows gates. |
| D02 | A real Swift/SwiftUI native Mac product is required. | Inspect and extend `native-app/`; do not relabel Electron or use a WebView as the entire “native” application. Keep the existing Mac Electron product stable until an explicit migration is qualified. |
| D03 | Public native Mac distribution is on hold pending Apple inputs. | Continue implementation and permitted local QA. Never publish an ad-hoc build as a trusted customer DMG, and never fabricate a placeholder DMG asset. |
| D04 | Every new 2.0 installation starts Cloudflare-speech-first and lean, including native Mac. | Default to the qualified Cloudflare-hosted speech route; do not bundle, fetch or load optional local speech/LLM/vision weights. Preserve explicit existing offline preferences through a visible migration, not silent cloud activation. |
| D05 | Jev is the preferred configured decision backend; Laya is a required alternative. | Both feed the same action/policy contracts. Neither supplies microphone capture, arbitrary prose, OS permissions or authorization by itself. |
| D06 | Native Apple inference is a capability-aware route. | Prefer supported Apple intelligence for eligible native workloads only after checking SDK, device, account, distribution, policy and quota availability. Do not assume PCC works in a public Developer ID DMG. |
| D07 | Shared organizational secrets stay server-side. | Operator owns Jev, hosted Laya service credentials, approved speech and generative credentials. Do not distribute organizational master keys in installers, preloads, logs or configuration. |
| D08 | The requested Libraries.dev visual language is required. | Use the solving mesh orb inside the command bar, the animated beam border and voice-responsive bottom glow. Respect reduced motion and the existing microphone owner. |
| D09 | Quality is measured, not promised as infallible. | “Best possible” means strong evaluation, truthful uncertainty, exact critical facts and no silent semantic rewriting—not a guarantee that all speech or actions can always be perfect. |
| D10 | Production metering and engineering token efficiency are separate. | Portal usage represents Métis users. Developer graph/compaction metrics represent coding work and must not inflate customer consumption. |
| D11 | Cloudflare is the default speech provider, initially `@cf/deepgram/nova-3`. | Implement the qualified live route end to end; Soniox/direct Deepgram are not automatic fallbacks. Model upgrades require quality, privacy and compatibility qualification. |
| D12 | Local speech and local generation are independently optional. | Settings shows compatibility, download/installed size and measured device readiness. Installation and selection require explicit user choice or signed/admin-trusted policy. |
| D13 | No durable speech or inference-content storage in Cloudflare. | Disable content logging/caching and disallowed storage sinks; confirm actual provider/route terms and deployed settings. Retain only the approved, minimized operational ledger. |
| D14 | Cloudflare's default remains a policy preference, not permission to capture or send data. | A cloud outage or privacy-readiness failure must never silently enable local models, another vendor, queued audio upload or recording. |
| D15 | The numbered plan is the implementation contract. | Each task has dependencies, required work and exit evidence; config files or mocked responses alone never mark deployment complete. |
| D16 | Mantu Intelligence is governed shared knowledge, not an infallible LLM. | Canonical source/revision/permission/provenance wins over generated text; wiki/graph is rebuildable and cannot self-certify. |
| D17 | Dust agents must read and make allowed canonical updates. | Personal/service authority is explicit; CAS/idempotency, human approval where required and durable readback prevent false success. |
| D18 | The normal Settings experience is small and clear. | Four everyday destinations, search and Advanced disclosure; preserve power features and migrate every key without weakening policy. |
| D19 | Platform skills are centrally versioned and executed. | Reuse the registry; clients receive metadata/results, no arbitrary local scripts or hidden cloud/offline substitution. |
| D20 | Métis remains an app and adds visible Teams surfaces/participation. | Personal tab, meeting panel and communications service are separately authenticated and qualified. |
| D21 | Automatic attendance covers eligible explicitly enrolled meetings at actual start. | No automatic recording everywhere, lobby bypass, recurring-instance confusion or rejoin after removal; other platforms are capability-gated. |
| D22 | Existing Cloudflare no-content-persistence remains. | Approved knowledge lives only in the selected organizational store; authored skill software is separately classified, never a loophole for private run content. |
| D23 | GDPR readiness is a deployment-specific controlled process. | Identity, notice, legal basis, parties, retention and rights require real evidence and owner approval; no vendor logo creates compliance. |



**“Codex 2” clarification:** Tony explicitly requests Codex-led development of the native Mac version for Métis 2.0. Do not invent a product, SDK, CLI flag or model named “Codex 2.” Record the actual authorized Codex application/CLI and model versions used. If that phrase identifies a specific environment unavailable to the agent, request its exact identifier while continuing independent work. It is not a reason to substitute a non-native port. [R15]

### 1.3 Operating rules

Plan before changing code, then execute safe, authorized milestones. Preserve uncommitted work, stable bundle/package IDs, TCC identity where applicable, license/seat identity, settings, notes, encryption keys and update continuity. Never reset or force-push to make an old plan match the working tree. Resolve the designated release branch from repository evidence and Tony's existing decision; do not silently choose a stale `release/*` name.

Separate behavior-changing repairs, new capabilities and behavior-preserving refactors into reviewable commits. Use disjoint worktrees for parallel workers and one integrator for shared IPC, schema, routing and release contracts. Do not let optional graph tooling delay a release-blocking safety or UX fix.

No new paid service, credential rotation, production database mutation, external message, destructive migration or public deployment is implied merely by writing this specification. Implement locally and in authorized staging; use existing approvals for consequential operations. Do not weaken branch protection, signing, consent or security tests to produce green results. An actual access failure must be reported with the failed operation, completed work and smallest unblock—not treated as permission to use another identity or bypass.

When capability evidence is missing, classify it as UNKNOWN or BLOCKED. When a request is unsupported, make that visible and retain typed/manual alternatives. Never create a broad `exec` tool that makes every request superficially “supported.”

## 2. Evidence baseline and first inspection

### 2.1 What has actually been reviewed

The repository is `mysticalsin/AskToto-Mantu`. The main reference was rechecked for this specification at `fff88c2686e2ce0d55c26d61413d948b34d2dc47`. The prior architecture audit compared that snapshot with PR #194 at `a105a258aab0cb9ab07b24dbf85538dce84b6de8`. These are reconnaissance anchors, not an instruction to overwrite newer work. The prior audit was targeted source inspection, not a complete production penetration test, native acceptance run or inspection of the deployed database. [R01, R02]

The preceding audit found real Electron/React, native prototype, Mantu Intelligence, Operator Worker/D1, speech, knowledge, authentication, policy, telemetry and packaging foundations. It also identified incomplete command verification, partial Jev wiring, portal aggregation/merge risks, optional embedded account credentials, policy/deployment gaps and unqualified user journeys. Two pinned resource inventories do list **1,567,510,751 bytes** of assets in total, about **1.57 decimal GB / 1.46 GiB**, before Electron, other helpers, application code and compression effects. ### 2.2 Mandatory first-pass inventory

Record branch, full source SHA, dirty files, installed/package version, native project targets, supported architectures, lockfiles, build profiles, release feeds, CI definitions, actual backend deployment identifiers and database migration level. Read applicable `AGENTS.md`, existing design and security rules, supplied refactoring skill and relevant tests. An unavailable skill is a named input gap, not permission to invent its contents.

Inspect the user-provided portal through an authenticated, authorized session:
`https://metis-operator.tony-walteur.workers.dev/?range=24h#overview`.
The URL is an entry point, not proof of accessible data. This specification did not establish its live authenticated state. Trace its frontend, Worker version, database binding, request routes and filters before attributing the user's incident to a source bug. Do not expose a protected portal or add fake production events to make screenshots look populated.

Build an audit coverage map with inspected, executed, sampled and unassessed paths. Run baseline typechecks/tests/build/security checks that are available; record real exit statuses, skips and environment. A test file that fails to transform did not “pass zero tests.” Reconcile existing findings and classify pre-existing failures independently from new regressions.

## 3. Target architecture and trust boundaries

### 3.1 Retain the foundations and make the paths explicit

```text
Windows React/Electron UX                 Native macOS SwiftUI UX
        | typed, restricted IPC                 | native app/XPC boundary
        +------------------+-------------------+
                           |
              Trusted audio + command coordinator
       wake detection / context / policy / approval / cancellation
                           |
       +-------------------+-------------------+
       |                   |                   |
  Deterministic       Operator gateway      Native Apple route
  local actions       authenticated APIs    eligible OS SDK only
       |              STT / Jev / Laya      on-device / PCC
       |              generative services   explicit policy exception
       +-------------------+-------------------+
                           |
            Validated action plan; no model authority
                  native / browser / MCP adapters
                           |
                Postcondition verification + receipt
                           |
          Metadata-only usage and operational ledger in Operator

User knowledge: approved summaries/actions → existing second brain / OneDrive
Install assets: reviewed immutable components → R2 → verified local activation
Engineering only: source graph + ADRs + tasks + relay; never the user-note graph
```

This is a proposed flow, not a claim all requests already use one gateway. The current product has multiple inference routes. Audit each route's authorization, credential owner, egress, retention, billing and policy behavior. In the managed enterprise profile, centrally funded API work must follow Operator. Explicitly approved on-device Apple inference and authenticated developer CLI modes are separate route classes, not hidden direct-key fallbacks.

PCC is called through the native Apple SDK on an eligible device; it is not an endpoint to proxy from a Cloudflare Worker for Windows users. Record allowed metadata afterward without funneling Apple-private prompts through Operator just for analytics. Laya's Python/Torch inference is an approved separately hosted service behind the gateway, not assumed to run in a standard Worker or R2 bucket.

### 3.1.1 Shared intelligence and meeting-service extension (target)

```text
Standalone Windows / Native Mac         Teams personal / meeting app
             |                                    |
             +---- Entra-validated service APIs --+
                                  |
               Operator policy + published skill control
                 metadata ledger (no meeting content)
                                  |
                 Governed Mantu Intelligence API <---- Dust remote MCP
                                  |
               Selected approved M365 knowledge records
                  canonical revisions + scoped projections

Eligible actual meeting start -> metadata-only join coordinator
 -> visible admitted assistant -> qualified Teams media receiver (Azure/.NET)
 -> authorized Cloudflare speech -> transient transcript / approved summary
 -> canonical knowledge -> scoped wiki/graph -> authorized skill / response
```

This diagram is a proposed separation of responsibilities, not proof of deployment. Teams media hosting and source-record storage do not become Cloudflare storage by being orchestrated by Operator. Cloudflare content restrictions remain. A Teams tab/Dust conversation cannot directly invoke privileged desktop APIs; the local user/session authorizes any local action separately.

### 3.2 Shared contracts, platform-native execution

Keep a compact versioned capability contract shared across TypeScript and Swift through generated schemas or a single schema source plus golden fixtures. Share identifiers, validation rules, policy semantics, request/attempt correlation and expected outcomes—not OS-specific implementation tricks. A browser URL request differs from launching an executable; an app instance differs from a window or tab.

Each capability definition must specify ID/version, permitted platforms, prerequisites, argument schema, data sensitivity, risk class, target resolution, required approval, cancellation behavior, idempotency/reconciliation behavior, timeout and independently observable success criteria. Unsupported capabilities are filtered before model selection.

Maintain separate counters and IDs for a user operation, a plan, a model attempt, an action step, a capture generation, a target snapshot and a usage event. An operation can contain several model attempts and several steps. No single ambiguous `askId` should perform every identity role.

### 3.3 Runtime execution contract

A plan contains only validated capability IDs and resolved candidates. Each step binds the authorized principal, device, policy revision, target identity, relevant snapshot revision, parameters, expiry and exact approval scope. Before committing a side effect, recheck that the session is live, the target is still the authorized target, policy still permits it and approval has not expired or changed. This validation belongs in trusted execution code, not an LLM prompt.

Return a typed result with `attempted`, `verified`, `failed`, `cancelled`, `unsupported` or `unknown`, plus a safe reason code and permitted evidence references. A process returning exit code zero establishes at most its command's own success contract; it does not automatically establish a window, document or remote transaction postcondition.

## 4. The command-session contract

### 4.1 One audio owner; explicit privacy modes

Implement or extend a trusted audio broker. Command capture must be owned by the main/native boundary or a qualified native helper, not granted authority because an untrusted renderer submits PCM tagged “you.” Validate IPC sender, lifecycle and session ownership. Document the remaining threat model: this does not magically authenticate a human voice or defeat arbitrary same-user malware.

Use one microphone capture with authorized consumers where the OS/architecture permits. Meeting transcription and command capture remain logically distinct. Ending a command does not stop a separately authorized meeting; stopping meeting transcription does not secretly leave full cloud command transcription running.

Expose three unambiguous controls: **wake detection enabled**, **command microphone off**, and **stop all capture**. Command-off releases its subscription and disables wake/command processing; if an explicitly enabled meeting still uses the microphone, say so. Stop-all stops microphone, system-audio and command consumers and revokes pending command authority. Do not label a device “microphone off” while another Métis feature is still capturing.

Idle wake detection is opt-in, minimal and on-device. Use a small reviewed wake detector/OS capability and a bounded volatile buffer. No idle audio, recognized ambient conversation or rolling command transcript goes to Jev, Laya, analytics or cloud STT. After a detected wake boundary, transfer only the authorized command interval; handle the first command syllable without uploading the entire pre-wake buffer. Keep a tested policy for the small boundary margin and record it in privacy documentation.

A local wake detector is not a new general-purpose local ASR fallback. The default cloud-only speech profile remains cloud-only; choosing optional local speech is an explicit mode/policy transition. Microphone permissions need a real first-use gesture; importing a UI effect or hovering over the panel may never request a microphone.

### 4.2 Explicit states and transitions

| State | Allowed work | Visible result / exit rule |
|---|---|---|
| OFF | No command capture, wake model or cloud stream. | Static/hidden control; keyboard/text remains available. |
| ARMED | Authorized local wake detection only. | Only the mesh orb: no pill, caption, glow, badge or label. Accessible button and keyboard focus outline remain; see §5.11. |
| CAPTURING | Post-wake command STT and local level metering. | Bar appears, live caption above it, voice glow reacts. |
| INTERPRETING | Resolve intent/candidates; bounded provider decision. | Solving orb; preserve provisional text and cancellation. |
| AWAITING_APPROVAL | Present exact target/action and consequence. | Stable focusable approval card; no side effect yet. |
| EXECUTING | Commit one authorized step; monitor cancellation. | Brief meaningful action feedback, not theatrical delays. |
| VERIFYING | Read the expected target state. | Do not show successful completion before proof. |
| COMPLETED | Verified result, permitted receipt metadata. | Concise confirmation; return to armed/off by policy. |
| FAILED / UNKNOWN | Safe explanation and recovery choices. | Distinguish a failed attempt from an uncertain side effect. |
| CANCELLED | Stop future steps, revoke generation and abort supported work. | State what already happened, if anything; no automatic destructive undo. |

Use a monotonic session generation. Late STT/model/helper events from a stopped/replaced owner cannot revive a command, update another session or commit another side effect. Expiry, sign-out, policy revocation, permission removal, suspend and device replacement must all have explicit transitions.

### 4.3 Fast preparation is not premature action

Partial speech can prepare candidates, prefetch non-sensitive app metadata and warm an approved connection. It must not execute a side effect simply because a partial sentence sounds actionable. Test “open Chrome—actually, don't,” “close this tab, not the browser,” self-correction, hesitation, negation and a delayed final STT revision. Apply semantic stability and end-of-command rules appropriate to risk.

For low-risk, already authorized operations, commit once a complete stable intent and target exist. For text insertion or multi-step commands, bind exact confirmed text/context. High-impact actions require explicit target-specific UI confirmation; spoken “yes” alone is not sufficient identity or authorization. No confidence threshold bypasses this rule.

Typed commands use the same plan and execution contracts. They require a deliberate submit gesture but not microphone access. Meeting transcript lines, web content, subtitles, remote participants and assistant speech are never interpreted as command authority.

### 4.4 Cancellation and graceful close

Stop/Escape on the active surface must act locally and promptly without waiting for Jev or network timeouts. Provide a configurable global stop chord that does not capture common application shortcuts. A command to close an app first resolves the exact process/window and uses graceful close. Unsaved-work prompts remain visible and stop the automatic chain. Force quit is a distinct explicitly confirmed recovery action, never the default “close.”

Handle cancellation at the execution commit point. Do not report “cancelled, nothing happened” after an irreversible step has already committed. Reconcile an unknown result before retrying. A new session must not duplicate an old in-flight write merely because the UI was reopened.

### 4.5 Exact intent, manual interference and cross-surface stop

Treat direct low-risk requests as useful authority within the already authorized local session; do not demand repetitive approval for a clearly requested harmless open/focus action. Resolve ambiguity before close. Bind camera-app activation and capture to current explicit intent **before** preview can begin; retain required OS permission and exact consequence review without re-asking an already satisfied identical confirmation. This does not authorize background capture or photo upload.

A second deliberate identical command in a new activation is a new operation. Repeated callbacks within one activation are deduplicated. Bind pronouns such as “there” and “that note” to verified outputs from the preceding step, not the most convenient foreground object. Pause and re-resolve when the user changes focus, edits the target or takes control; never fight the user for keyboard/mouse control.

**Local stop and remote stop are different acknowledgments.** Local Stop immediately revokes the local command generation and stops its permitted capture consumers. A control labelled Stop all assistance also requests shutdown of authorized remote meeting/skill sessions and displays per-session acknowledgment. During a network partition, say remote stop unconfirmed; do not claim a bot has left because the local animation stopped. Each remote session has a server-enforced revocable lease and a reviewed finite lifetime. Test dropped stop messages, broker isolation, lease expiry and reconnect; never resume a stopped occurrence without new authority. The local p95 100 ms target is not a guarantee about a remote platform's leave latency.

On lock, sign-out, user switch or loss of trusted identity, revoke local privileged authority and suppress confidential previews. Explicit server-side meeting enrollment has separate lifetime/stop rules visible to the user; locking a laptop does not silently terminate or authorize every remote service. Preserve tested Private View/content-protection semantics without promising an OS can prevent every external capture.

## 5. Voice/orb/bar experience and simplified Settings

### 5.1 Visual contract

When expanded, the command surface is a calm dark pill, not a dashboard or a floating developer demo. ARMED is only the mesh orb (§5.11), not this pill. Place the **mesh orb inside the left side of the bar**. Use the Libraries.dev **solving** animation while deciding, preparing and verifying; use listening for active capture and a restrained resting state otherwise. Preserve the animated colored beam around the border and a sound-responsive glow at the bottom. The command transcription floats **above** the pill in a small readable caption card. Do not display a redundant decorative “Solving,” “Thinking” or library-demo label beside the orb. [R05–R07]

```text
          ┌───────────────────────────────────────────┐
          │ Hey Métis, open my latest meeting notes.   │  ← real live caption
          └───────────────────────────────────────────┘
    ╭───────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────╮
    │  [mesh orb]  Ask Métis or continue by typing…      [stop/send] │
    ╰──────────────── colorful animated beam ──────────────────────╯
                     sound-reactive bottom glow
```

This constraint does not prohibit necessary consent, approval, error, missing-permission, download or verified-result feedback. Put those in the caption/result area or an expanded card. State must also be available to assistive technology; a silent glowing orb is not an accessible explanation of what the computer is doing.

Proposed sizing: expanded bar roughly 520–680 device-independent pixels wide and 76–92 high on a normal desktop; use the separately tuned 64px orb variant where it fits and 20px only for genuinely compact inline states. The true bounds adapt to the work area, scale, safe insets and user text size. Do not scale the entire UI to squeeze it into a narrow edge strip. Let long captions wrap to two or three lines, then provide expansion rather than covering controls or growing without bound.

### 5.2 Placement, focus and drafts

An ARMED right-edge surface must render only the fully visible mesh orb, with compact hit bounds; older collapsed-handle designs are superseded by §5.11. When activated, expand inward from the chosen display edge using the existing geometry authority. Preserve the vertical anchor as far as the work area permits. Keep caption, approval and result cards inside the same display's usable bounds. Re-anchor safely on monitor removal, scaling change, taskbar/Dock changes, negative coordinates and full-screen transitions.

The active command surface is temporarily pinned while recording a command, typing, composing via an IME, awaiting approval, executing or showing an unresolved error. Pointer departure cannot collapse it. Avoid unconditional focus theft for status updates; a requested app-open/focus action may deliberately change focus. A click to type must give the input focus without an animation delay or a native drag region intercepting the gesture.

Persist the draft across ordinary collapse/reopen and answer streaming; clear it only through a deliberate clear/submit flow with defined history behavior. A stopped command's transient speech text must not become a permanent transcript archive. Maintain local accessible focus on errors and approvals without replaying every streamed character to screen readers.

### 5.3 Expanded Ask, meeting and Intelligence surfaces

Repair the right-edge panel using definite-height containers where child views own scrolling. Keep the answer scroller separate from the composer and toolbar. Test long URLs, unbroken identifiers, markdown tables, code blocks, attachments, multilingual text and small window heights. Essential actions remain discoverable; hide-on-hover text or overlapping icons is not acceptable. Use responsive grouping/menus for secondary actions rather than shrinking targets indefinitely.

Retain top-center, existing Hide/Island/Bar and the approved dock modes. Keep the settings label, preview and actual geometry consistent; prevent unsupported placement/style combinations at schema and UI boundaries. Show clear loading, offline, authorization, unsupported and empty states in desktop, native Mac, Intelligence and Operator—not a perpetual spinner.

### 5.4 Motion system

Use motion to acknowledge and explain, never to hold work back. Proposed transitions: wake reveal 100–160ms, collapse 120–180ms, short result dwell configurable. Execution begins when authorized, not when an animation finishes. Do not imply latency is zero by hiding network work.

All motion follows reduced-motion settings and suspends when hidden or offscreen. Avoid multiple independent requestAnimationFrame loops, unnecessary rerenders per sample and continuous GPU use while idle. Share a clock or the library's intended internal mechanism, drive microphone level through a ref/getter or qualified native observable, and avoid React state updates for every audio sample. Tone down or pause effects under energy-saving policies.

### 5.5 Required visual evidence

Capture the running Windows and native Mac applications at the same defined states: resting, wake capture, partial caption, solving, target clarification, approval, executing, verified result, failure, microphone off, network unavailable and reduced motion. Include right-edge and top-center, light/dark, 100/150/200% Windows scaling and representative Mac display settings. Capture a real uncut command video with visible timings and no synthetic actions. Keep simulated design material labelled separately.

### 5.6 Simplify Settings: four everyday destinations, one advanced drawer

The product goal is confidence with few decisions, not a hidden panel full of the same confusing switches. Keep four everyday destinations: **General; Voice & meetings; Knowledge & skills; Privacy & account**. Put **Advanced** behind a labelled disclosure and Settings search. This replaces the earlier standalone Speech/AI navigation labels; the independent local-speech and local-generation capabilities remain. Do not add a fifth top-level page whenever a backend gains a parameter.

| Destination | Everyday controls | Progressive disclosure / operator-owned controls |
|---|---|---|
| General | Appearance, placement, launch behavior, language, shortcuts and accessibility. | Show only compatible placement/style combinations. Keep debugging and raw geometry out of normal setup. |
| Voice & meetings | Microphone, test sound, Cloudflare speech status, wake activation, automatic meeting participation and a clear pause/skip control. | Optional local speech, supported languages, per-meeting exceptions. Vendor endpoints, budgets and raw STT tuning belong in Operator. |
| Knowledge & skills | Selected knowledge space, sharing audience, sync health, available skills and favorite actions. | Offline/online limitations, source connections, review permissions and local disk usage. Skill authoring/publishing is in Operator. |
| Privacy & account | Entra account/organization, capture controls, effective storage/retention, connected apps, export/delete requests and sign-out. | Show managed policy with its owner and explanation; a policy lock is not a broken switch. |
| Advanced drawer | Optional local generation and vision, approved personal/developer integrations, diagnostics, update/component repair. | Compatibility-tested alternatives only. No master account keys or controls that loosen enterprise policy. |

Default experience: Cloudflare selected, no local model downloads, one visible readiness summary, and no provider shopping exercise. Normal users should not need to know what a gateway ID, diarization engine, temperature or context window is. Preserve permitted expert features under search/disclosure rather than deleting them. Security-critical controls, stop capture and privacy disclosures must never be buried in Advanced.

### 5.7 Settings model, migration and consistency

Build one schema-driven settings registry containing stable key, plain label, help text, owner (user/device/organization), effective value, readiness, visibility predicate, dependencies, validation and legacy mapping. Render different platform views from the same semantics. A control is selected, allowed and ready independently. Avoid two stores that can disagree about the same choice.

For every old option, classify it as KEEP, MERGE, ADVANCED, OPERATOR-ONLY, DEPRECATED or PLATFORM-SPECIFIC, with the migration and regression test. Preserve explicit user choices and machine-trusted policy. Do not reset local-only to cloud-first during cleanup or expose a disabled enterprise feature through an old deep link. Deleted aliases need a deliberate versioned migration, not a silent parse fallback.

Simple reversible appearance changes may apply immediately; policy, capture, sharing, model installation and other consequential changes require the appropriate explicit action. Show saving/saved/failed states and revert or preserve a recoverable draft on failure. A toggle may not remain visually on after the backend rejected it. Server changes identify policy revision and take effect at a safe session boundary; emergency revocation acts immediately.

Use Settings search with synonyms such as microphone, offline, local, record, Teams, privacy and storage. Search only controls the principal may see; do not reveal hidden organizational secrets or another tenant's configuration. Provide direct links to the correct nested control, a consistent back action and readable inline errors. Preserve keyboard focus and IME composition during searches and value updates.

### 5.8 Interaction concepts to implement, not mock data to ship

The following layouts are requirements sketches. The production UI must show real state, not these example labels as permanent green statuses.

```text
MÉTIS SETTINGS                          Search settings

General              Voice & meetings
Voice & meetings     Speech processing    Cloudflare · [actual readiness]
Knowledge & skills   Microphone           Selected device · Test
Privacy & account    Hey Métis            Off / On (explicit opt-in)
                     Meeting assistant    Manual / Automatic for eligible meetings
Advanced >           Today                [actual meetings, eligibility and Skip]
                     Optional local speech >  Not installed · Review compatibility

                     Stop all capture      [always reachable]
                     Managed by your organization · View effective policy
```

```text
MANTU INTELLIGENCE                     My space / Approved team space

Ask about your work                    Sources and permissions
“What changed on this account?”        Updated: [real freshness]

Verified records | Needs review | Conflicts | Stale sources

Answer / account briefing              Evidence drawer
- What changed                         Source record + revision + date
- Open commitments                     Verified / extracted / inferred / disputed
- Unknowns and next action              Correction history and authorized editor

Timeline | Meetings | Actions | People & accounts | Graph (advanced)
```

```text
OPERATOR · SKILLS                      New skill / Import reviewed definition

Catalog     Drafts     Review     Published     Run health
Skill       Owner      Version   Audience      Quality       Usage
[actual configured entries only; no seeded customer-looking success figures]

Preview typed inputs → Review context access → Run synthetic test → Publish
Recent runs: version, outcome, latency, known consumption, completeness
No private meeting text or unrestricted conversation replay in admin analytics
```

### 5.9 Smoothness and usability qualification

Use restrained Mantu visual tokens, readable spacing, native-feeling controls, platform-appropriate typography and the existing orb language. The Apple-like goal is coherent decisions and predictable feedback, not copying Apple branding or making Windows behave like an unfamiliar Mac port. Test light/dark/high-contrast, keyboard-only use, screen-reader announcements, scaling, touch targets where supported and reduced motion.

Proposed release study: at least eight representative nontechnical participants complete five tasks—change microphone, pause automatic attendance, inspect privacy, enable an allowed offline pack and find an available skill. Target at least 90% unassisted task completion, no privacy-critical misunderstanding, and no more than two navigation transitions for ordinary controls. Record failures and time distributions; small studies are design evidence, not universal proof. Include actual Windows, native Mac and the constrained Teams side-panel layout.

Capture before/after option inventory and screen recordings. Do not declare simplicity from fewer visible labels while moving necessary explanations into tooltips or removing functionality. Navigation and search must work with the actual allowed capabilities, pending configuration and offline states.

### 5.10 CodeNotch continuity and one geometry owner

Retain the CodeNotch-inspired behavior as a reference, not a dependency application: inward expansion, hover hysteresis, click-to-pin/unpin, explicit keyboard recovery, normalized per-display vertical position and reset/recenter. Hover alone never records, calls a model or acts. In ARMED it also never expands the orb; §5.11 supersedes idle hover-reveal. Preserve existing placement when the feature is disabled. Treat native macOS and Electron coordinate systems separately at an explicit boundary; do not multiply device-independent bounds by scale indiscriminately. A right-side Dock/taskbar collision needs a stable tested policy, not competing bounds loops. Transparent passive window regions must not block unrelated application input. [R72, R75]

### 5.11 Orb-only ARMED state — animated solving, one interaction owner

**Latest owner decision:** ARMED is just the animated solving orb. This supersedes revision 4.1/r6’s static-idle decision, not the orb-only geometry or safety rules. No idle bar, placeholder, caption, badge, outside glow, decorative label or result card. A focused button may have the necessary accessible focus outline. Meeting/OS capture indicators remain independently visible when their own processing is active.

Use the requested Libraries.dev solving geometry and preset, not a rotating bitmap, generic particle network or an imitation “breathing” spinner. The 64 avatar preset and device-pixel backing store remain distinct from the visible host size. The uploaded DESIGN.md specifies a 41px production Bar host; preserve/qualify that existing size through the UI inventory rather than silently treating this lab’s enlarged 64px visual and 72px hit target as a production requirement. Specify separate visual bounds, accessible hit target and actual native window/input region. Never pass an unsupported size value to the library.

The default visible, unlocked ARMED state animates while ordinary motion is allowed. Use a shared motion policy: reduced-motion, explicit pause, hidden/offscreen, OS lock and approved low-power constraints stop or simplify decorative animation without changing capture permission. The local animation is not proof of AI activity and causes no model/audio request. Maintain a stable clock across state transitions; do not use per-frame React state. The prototype uses a bounded 30fps idle draw target; qualify actual battery/frame pacing on native devices. No arbitrary CPU threshold is claimed from the preview.

Click or Enter/Space on the orb opens typed input without arming a microphone. A trusted opt-in wake opens the full surface: live caption above, orb inside, animated beam and genuine input-level glow. Hover never starts capture, inference or a device action. Typing/composition and approval stay protected. A dormant draft is parked separately during a new voice session and restored exactly; command text must never silently include that draft. Busy/replayed/stale/locked wake events cannot restart a committed action. OFF plus typing stays OFF when dismissed.

The expanded bar does not disappear while an approval, active composition or unknown outcome still needs attention. Escape respects IME and active surface focus; the native global Stop is separately implemented. Shrink the real native input region after closing, not only CSS opacity. Verify outside-orb clicks reach the underlying application without an invisible rectangular blocker. Screen reader names are allowed; no “Solving” word is painted beside the orb.

The offline HTML now contains an attributed JavaScript adaptation of the upstream solving frame algorithm and preset. This is stronger visual context than the previous approximation, but it is not the installed React package, compiled native port or live speech integration. TASK-030 must qualify the actual lockfile/API and native implementation against this geometry and the linked upstream source. Platform behavior remains a native test gate.


## 6. React and native rendering integration

### 6.1 React components

Inspect the installed lockfile first: the prior root manifest already included `thinking-orbs`. Reuse or deliberately update that dependency rather than install a second copy. Verify package provenance, licenses, exports, React compatibility, transitive dependencies and bundle impact before adding `border-beam` or `voice-glow`. Pin reviewed versions; no unreviewed `@latest`, CDN code or remote runtime component loading in production.

Use the supplied component APIs as the **expanded-surface** integration target, subject to checking the selected package version. Before mounting this branch, render the isolated accessible orb button for ARMED (see §5.11 and `integration/MetisCommandSurface.tsx` in the current kit). OFF/hidden rendering must not masquerade as ARMED:

```tsx
import { ThinkingOrb } from 'thinking-orbs';
import { BorderBeam } from 'border-beam';
import { VoiceBeam } from 'voice-glow';

// Structural integration sketch, not a drop-in tested component.
// `session`, `draft`, `submit`, `stop` and `levelRef` belong to Métis's
// existing qualified controller. This component owns no microphone.
const working = ['interpreting', 'executing', 'verifying'].includes(session.phase);
const orbState = working ? 'solving' : session.phase === 'capturing' ? 'listening' : 'breathing';
const animate = session.visible && !reducedMotion && !energyPolicy.pauseEffects;

<section aria-label="Métis command" data-command-session={session.id}>
  <CommandCaption text={session.displayTranscript} />
  <BorderBeam size="md" colorVariant="colorful" strength={0.7}
              theme={theme} active={animate}>
    <VoiceBeam level={() => levelRef.current}
               processing={working} active={animate} paused={!animate}
               colorVariant="colorful" theme={theme}>
      <div className="metis-command-pill">
        <span aria-hidden="true">
          <ThinkingOrb state={orbState} size={64} dark={theme === 'dark'}
                       paused={!animate} />
        </span>
        <CommandComposer value={draft} onChange={setDraft}
                         onSubmit={submit} disabled={session.awaitingApproval} />
        <CommandStopButton onClick={stop} />
      </div>
    </VoiceBeam>
  </BorderBeam>
  <CommandStatusAnnouncement state={session.accessibleStatus} />
</section>
```

Do not paste the Libraries.dev `useMicrophone()` demo into the product. Supply the existing trusted stream or, preferably for the view boundary, a sanitized scalar level from the audio broker. Clamp malformed/non-finite levels to a safe range. Apply the correct mute/off state to the meter; a CSS animation must never claim to be responding to audio that is not being captured. Remove animation-only labels from visual layout while retaining a throttled accessible status. Validate Enter, Shift+Enter, IME commit, paste, screen-reader focus and the stop button with real component tests.

### 6.2 Native equivalents are available for part of the effect set

The inspected Libraries.dev Swift package for ThinkingOrbsKit declares macOS 12 support; BorderBeamKit declares macOS 14 and bundles a Metal shader resource that its manifest says must be compiled through Xcode. These are useful source-backed starting points, not proof that the Métis native target has compiled them. [R08, R09]

Integrate those reviewed packages into the native target when compatible. Build/test actual shader resources, sandbox behavior, high contrast, reduced motion, display scaling and GPU use. Do not create a WKWebView or ship React/Electron solely to render a native orb. A comparable native voice glow may need a small SwiftUI/Canvas/Metal adaptation if no qualified port exists; keep it visual-only, licensed, and driven by the same trusted scalar level. Do not label an approximation “the exact library implementation.”

Preserve one semantic session model across platforms even if rendering differs. The Mac native app uses AppKit/SwiftUI windows and genuine native focus/accessibility semantics. Windows keeps the existing React/Electron surface and qualified native bridges. Pixel-identical rendering is less important than faithful motion, accessibility, reliable input and matching outcomes.

## 7. Performance budgets and instant-feeling interaction

These are **proposed launch budgets**, not measured results or vendor promises. Before enforcing them, record the reference hardware, OS, display, power state, network, service region, concurrency and warm/cold distinction. Change a budget only with a documented reason and owner approval; do not improve the chart by excluding failed or slow attempts.

| Measurement | Proposed acceptance target | Boundary |
|---|---|---|
| Wake event → first visible command frame | p95 ≤150ms | Local detector event to painted UI; includes main/renderer or native handoff. |
| Local stop gesture → session authority revoked | p95 ≤100ms | Does not promise reversal of a side effect already committed. |
| Speech interval end → first partial containing that interval | p95 ≤700ms | Use aligned audio offsets; a live partial need not wait for an entire utterance. Report language/device/network and accuracy separately. |
| Stable command → prepared decision | p95 ≤800ms on warm approved service | Includes gateway, network and validation; deterministic path reported separately. |
| Stable simple command → execution dispatch | p95 ≤1.2s | Valid target, no new consent, ready adapter; do not bypass permission/approval. |
| Warm supported open/focus action → verified result | p95 ≤2s after stable command | Cold app launch and slow sites measured separately, not hidden. |
| Cached local UI navigation | p95 ≤100ms response | No network gate for opening the local panel/settings. |
| Hidden idle CPU | Goal ≤1% of one logical CPU averaged over 5 minutes | Total Métis processes, wake detector included when enabled; define sampling. |
| Cloud-first idle memory | Goal ≤350MiB Windows process tree; ≤180MiB native Mac | No optional local models loaded; include helpers and GPU-process accounting separately. |
| First useful cloud-first setup | Goal ≤90s on documented 40Mbps connection/reference device | Measure core acquisition, validation and readiness; separate user sign-in/permission waiting. |
| Animation responsiveness | No recurring long-task/jank regression at reference settings | Profile real orb/beam/voice effects, not only a static placeholder. |

Measure p50/p95/p99, failure rate and sample counts. Use monotonic clocks locally; correlate client/server spans without subtracting unsynchronized clocks. Track time to first paint, wake recognition, STT partial/final, policy, decision, dispatch, external completion, verification and result rendering separately.

Optimize in this order: eliminate unnecessary work; reuse ready instances and static app metadata; batch compatible typed decisions; reduce context to necessary observed state; reuse connections under policy; avoid loading optional models; lazy-load expensive views; eliminate competing layout and audio loops. Do not send one paid decision on every keystroke or every partial transcript. Coalesce duplicates, cancel superseded inference where safe, and cap inference cost per session.

Warm an authorized connection or preload a small dependency only when justified. Warm-up is not permission to begin recording, upload ambient audio, inspect private windows or download gigabytes. Never delay a completed authorized action to synchronize it with a “beautiful” animation.

### 7.1 Capacity, fairness and predictable degradation

Before claiming enterprise scale, record an explicit deployment envelope: registered devices, peak simultaneously active users, concurrent command/meeting streams, average and p95 meeting length, per-track bitrate, provider quotas, skill fan-out, knowledge size, target regions and failure headroom. Use owner-approved numbers or labelled planning assumptions; a 12,000-person organization is not automatically 12,000 simultaneous audio streams. Measure a representative load and a controlled overload condition against the same fidelity and privacy rules.

One centrally funded key creates shared capacity. Use a coordinator appropriate to the actual server deployment, not only process-local counters. Enforce per-principal fairness plus global budget/concurrency limits, prioritize interactive commands over rebuilds, and guarantee background progress within its declared service class. Reject or queue **metadata-only** work before accepting expensive content when capacity is exhausted. No unbounded audio backlog, per-keystroke paid decision storm or hidden duplicate vendor race is an acceptable scaling strategy.

Separate user operation, provider attempt, action commit and accounting event. A vendor retry may still cost money when its output is discarded. A hard budget promise requires a justified upper-bound reservation or enforced quota at the billed boundary; if final vendor cost cannot be strictly bounded, state the approved exposure instead of calling a soft dashboard threshold a hard cap. Record actual tariff, retry and hosting costs rather than copying historical example prices.

## 8. Complete launch use-case catalog

This is the launch catalog and the template for extending it—not a claim that every conceivable app can be automated. Every row gets a real test ID and a platform result: VERIFIED, UNSUPPORTED, BLOCKED or NOT TESTED. A feature cannot be advertised as available merely because its row exists.

**Risk modes:** OBSERVE = bounded read-only work; ALLOWED = low-risk user-authorized operation within an active session; CONFIRM = exact consequence/target approval before side effects; BLOCKED = automatic execution must be refused. These are policy defaults, not model-selected authority. Organization policy may tighten them.

All desktop/browser rows require both Windows and native macOS verification unless an explicitly named platform difference is shown. Core browser launch/navigation can use an approved OS handler. Fine-grained tab/DOM actions require a qualified browser adapter or explicit controlled session; do not attach silently to arbitrary personal profiles or expose a public debugging port.

### 8.1 Activation and session safety

| ID | User scenario | Minimum observable acceptance | Risk |
|---|---|---|---|
| UC-001 | Say “Hey Métis.” | Start from orb-only ARMED; bar appears; only authorized post-wake command capture begins. | ALLOWED |
| UC-002 | Wake and immediately say “open Notes.” | First syllable is retained and exactly one command is planned. | ALLOWED |
| UC-003 | Type instead of speaking. | Same policy/verification path executes without microphone access. | ALLOWED |
| UC-004 | Speak an unrelated sentence during a meeting. | No desktop action, even if transcript arrives on a track labelled “you.” | BLOCKED |
| UC-005 | Say “open Chrome—actually, do not.” | Speculative preparation is discarded; no premature app launch. | BLOCKED |
| UC-006 | Cancel while the model is responding. | Authority is revoked locally; delayed responses do not execute. | ALLOWED |
| UC-007 | Use command-mic-off / stop-all. | The selected capture consumers stop; any remaining meeting capture is disclosed. | ALLOWED |
| UC-008 | Wake through media playback, echo or a remote speaker. | Adversarial/replayed input never bypasses consequential-action approval. | BLOCKED |

| UC-016 | Open an app not installed or unavailable on this platform. | Show unsupported/missing state; offer an explicit alternative, never silent substitution. | OBSERVE |

| ID | User scenario | Minimum observable acceptance | Risk |

For every row, add prerequisites, exact Windows/macOS capability IDs, supported application versions, negative tests, evidence location and owner in the repository test catalog. A named app/OS equivalent must be deliberately chosen. Mobile control references are assessed for patterns only; remote-phone control is not an implicit Métis 2.0 desktop release dependency. Unknown requests are handled by clarification, an explicit manual path or safe refusal—not by improvising unrestricted execution.

These extend the original catalog rather than replacing it. Record independent Windows, native Mac and any maintained Electron Mac results for each supported profile. Do not advertise a profile as ready because its UI selection exists.

## 9. Transcription fidelity, Cloudflare-first speech and evaluation

### 9.1 Turn “irrefutable” into a rigorous contract

Do not promise infallible or legally certified transcription. The product must preserve what was actually said as faithfully as supported evidence allows, make uncertainty actionable, and avoid substituting fluent invented wording for imperfect recognition. More confident-looking prose is not a quality improvement.

Separate four representations: transient audio; provisional/final speech-recognition segments; explicitly user-corrected text; and generated summaries/actions. Give segments stable IDs, revision numbers, monotonic audio offsets, language/source metadata and available confidence provenance. Generated content never overwrites raw recognition invisibly. Under the managed summary-only profile, the intermediate transcript can be shown during the session but must not become a new durable archive. Raising quality does not authorize retaining more data.

### 9.2 Capture correctness before changing models

Audit the audio path end to end: actual sample rate, channel count, buffer framing, resampling, clipping, silence, device gain, missing frames, clock drift, suspend, reconnect and final-buffer flush. Keep microphone and system-audio sources separate when available. Do not confuse “speaker from the local channel” with a verified named person.

Avoid accidental double capture, duplicated system audio, echo loops and sampling-rate headers that disagree with PCM bytes. Preserve a tail sufficient for final words; prove that endpointing does not cut numbers or negations. Track explicit gap events and reconnect offsets instead of concatenating discontinuous segments as continuous speech. Handle cloud partial/final replacement idempotently; duplicate or out-of-order provider events must not repeat words.

Only collect the audio necessary for an explicitly active feature. Bound queues by duration and memory; when overloaded, surface a gap and apply backpressure/recovery rather than retaining unbounded audio. Audio levels for the visual effect derive from this capture path, not a second microphone hook.

### 9.3 Provider qualification rather than marketing rankings

Use Cloudflare-hosted Nova-3 as the launch speech baseline. Soniox is a retained explicit, organization-approved alternative where configured; it is not a co-default or silent failover. Cloudflare documents Nova-3's real-time/batch interfaces, but that does not establish which service is most accurate on Tony's meetings. Qualify the selected Cloudflare model against current official API/version, language, residency, retention, diarization, vocabulary, streaming, outage and cost evidence. Alternative-model evaluations are scoped and authorized; do not send customer content to a comparison service by default. [R16]

Run one common harness over the same permitted audio and scoring rules. Report warm/cold and streaming/final quality separately. Do not select a provider from a generic leaderboard alone, or compare one vendor's live partials with another's offline final transcript. Benchmark Apple Speech on supported native devices only after explicit local-speech selection and policy approval; the cloud-only profile cannot silently switch to local recognition.

Second-pass correction is optional and bounded: use still-authorized volatile audio, a limited uncertain segment and available contextual vocabulary. It must not require indefinite audio retention or an expensive duplicate pass for every meeting. Any generative cleanup may format or propose a correction but cannot silently invent missing speech. If the source is unavailable after privacy cleanup, say that verification is no longer possible.

### 9.4 Evaluation corpus and metrics

Create a versioned, permissioned gold set with human-reviewed references. Proposed initial qualification: at least 10 hours across 20 distinct consenting speakers, with separate clean, realistic-noisy, accented, overlapping and code-switching subsets. Start with English and French, then Spanish and Portuguese according to the product's supported-language claims. Include other supported languages before advertising comparable performance. Keep a holdout split by speaker/meeting, not random neighboring audio chunks.

Include realistic technical and commercial vocabulary, dates, currencies, percentages, names, acronyms, addresses, interrupted sentences, disfluencies, negation, uncertain commitments and mixed-language entity names. Keep silent/no-speech, music, poor connections and clipped input cases. Do not use private employee/client recordings without the necessary authorization; synthetic audio supplements rather than replaces the real acoustic cases.

| Quality dimension | Proposed target / gate | Required interpretation |
|---|---|---|
| Clean, supported-language final WER | Goal ≤5% per release-qualified primary slice | Report substitutions, deletions, insertions, sample size and uncertainty; no claim for every recording. |
| Realistic-noisy final WER | Goal ≤10% for the defined benchmark slice | Difficult overlap/acoustics reported separately, not silently excluded from the published scope. |
| Critical facts | Target ≥99% exact accuracy on the curated names/numbers/dates/negation set | Report false-confident errors separately; a pass cannot be obtained by replacing every entity with “uncertain.” |
| Silent/no-speech input | Zero fabricated utterances in the release adversarial suite | Report duration and cases; zero observed failures is not proof of zero population risk. |
| Speaker/channel attribution | Quantified channel accuracy and diarization error where applicable | Anonymous speaker IDs until identity is known; no biometric identification by inference. |
| Streaming usability | Partial/final latency plus revision rate/flicker | A very fast unstable partial is not automatically a better transcript. |
| Commands | Exact intent/target/argument and negation accuracy | Wrong action has a different cost from punctuation; evaluate independently from meeting WER. |
| Summary/action fidelity | Human-scored factual consistency and source coverage | Do not score a polished summary as evidence that the underlying transcript was verbatim. |

These thresholds are proposed engineering targets. Freeze normalization rules before comparing runs; publish strict and normalized WER/CER where relevant. Report per-language/noise/device slices, not only an average that hides a failing group. Pre-register a practically acceptable non-inferiority margin for each primary slice and critical-error metric, then evaluate paired holdout results with confidence intervals or an appropriate clustered resampling method. A nonsignificant difference is not evidence of equivalence or non-inferiority. Report whether the study has sufficient evidence to meet the margin; otherwise retain an inconclusive gate. Where a target cannot be met, narrow the supported claim, fix the path or make uncertainty explicit; do not relabel the failure as success.

### 9.5 Quality UX and release evidence

Provide a visible uncertain-word/segment affordance without flooding the transcript with arbitrary confidence percentages. Allow correction and explain whether text is provisional, provider-final or edited. Preserve critical facts in exports and summaries. For a summary-only product, do not pretend an unavailable raw recording can later substantiate every sentence.

The release evidence includes exact provider/model/config versions, capture pipeline version, dataset hash and consent basis, normalization/scoring implementation, all primary slices, latency distribution, failures and before/after examples. Tests cover sign-out, stop-all, revoked consent, provider outage, retention cleanup and queue overload. The transcript feature may launch only for supported profiles that pass their stated gate.


### 9.6 Default routing and migration: implement this, not just the label

The default for every fresh Métis 2.0 Windows and native Mac profile is **Cloudflare-hosted speech**, initially `@cf/deepgram/nova-3` over its qualified real-time transport. Cloudflare lists this as a hosted model supporting batch and real-time use. Recheck model availability, exact endpoint/protocol/options, supported languages and tariff when pinning the release. Do not claim Nova-3 is best for every language without the benchmark. [R16, R45]

Keep one shared effective-speech resolver, with native fixtures for identical semantics. Separate three things: the user's selected engine, the engines allowed by organization policy, and which qualified engines are currently ready. A readiness failure cannot rewrite the selected engine. A local model being installed cannot make it selected. Having a modern Mac does not select Apple speech automatically. Native Apple generation/PCC remains a separate capability and cannot override this speech default.

Apply a versioned migration: preserve authenticated explicit offline/other-provider choices that policy still permits; explain a default migration to people who never selected an engine. Never begin uploading audio merely because an upgrade changed a default. Resolve ambiguous legacy settings through a visible choice. An organization-enforced mode wins, is visibly locked, and is not weakened by malformed policy or an empty settings field.

The inspected current `shouldUseCloudSttEngine()` returns false outside a cloud-only profile, even if a cloud provider is stored. Therefore changing only the dropdown/default string is insufficient. The credential resolver also expects a client-side Cloudflare token/account ID, and the live session opens both microphone and system tracks. Treat these as specific source migration targets, not evidence of a complete fleet-ready cloud default. [R49–R51]

**Preferred path:** authenticated desktop/native client → existing Operator speech-session broker → Cloudflare-hosted Workers AI speech → transient normalized result → the current authorized UI/command consumer. Account/provider credentials are injected only on the server. Retain the existing audio and segment implementation where correct; do not build a second speech application.

Distinguish `workers-ai/@cf/deepgram/nova-3` hosting from the AI Gateway provider-native `/deepgram` BYOK route. Sharing the word Deepgram or a Cloudflare gateway hostname does not make their operators, terms, transport, region, tariff or retention identical. The default is the explicitly approved **Cloudflare-hosted** route, not an opaque dynamic route that can forward elsewhere.

### 9.7 Server setup, session security and actual readiness

Codex must inspect the existing authenticated device/seat protocol and extend it, not send a shared ingestion secret as a universal bearer token. Define the required speech routes in the current router with versioned schemas and document their actual names after implementation. Authenticate and authorize before any model connection or paid work. Derive principal/organization/device on the server; reject payload claims that try to select another user, account, model, gateway, destination or recording scope.

Reuse a qualified Workers AI binding where it supports the required real-time protocol and verifiable privacy controls; otherwise implement a minimal authenticated Worker relay to the documented upstream WebSocket. This is a transport implementation choice, not permission to route content around Operator. A server-issued opaque session grant, when needed, is short-lived, scoped to device, operation, capture generation, permitted tracks/model and budget, and cannot be replayed into another session. Do not put account tokens in the renderer, URLs, WebSocket subprotocols, logs, installer or application preferences. Do not claim a grant is single-use unless its redemption is enforced atomically.

The server must verify privacy readiness, entitlement, rate/concurrency limits and budget reservation before accepting audio. Reject unauthenticated/unauthorized sockets, cross-origin portal requests where applicable, oversized/malformed frames and attempts to add unapproved tracks. Bound duration and bytes; revalidate authority at defined safe intervals and close sessions promptly on revocation. An open WebSocket is not an unlimited capability after sign-out.

Show separate readiness states: policy loaded, device authorized, cloud service reachable, speech configuration qualified, microphone allowed, selected tracks ready and transcript received. HTTP 200, successful socket upgrade, or a synthetic text response does not prove actual speech recognition.
