Probe with non-sensitive synthetic audio only after privacy configuration is in place, then prove authorized native capture. Do not make an expensive speech inference every time Settings opens; cache short-lived non-content readiness metadata with policy/config/version invalidation.

### 9.8 Fast, purpose-specific audio sessions

Command sessions are **microphone-only** by default. Meeting sessions open only the tracks requested and permitted by the user. Never open a system-audio speech connection for a simple command or submit a duplicate stream to obtain a cosmetic second speaker. A live meeting and a command must have separate authority, routing and retention scopes even when they share one trusted microphone owner. Reusing an existing authorized stream for a command requires an explicit command subscription and stable segment boundaries; meeting output does not itself acquire action authority.

Use native/AudioWorklet capture off the UI thread, an actual validated sample format, binary audio frames and bounded backpressure. The existing 16 kHz PCM16 path is a starting point, not permission to relabel 48 kHz samples. Preserve sample count and monotonic source offsets through resampling. Batch frames enough to avoid per-sample IPC/network overhead; begin evaluation around 20–80 ms frame windows and justify the selected value on representative hardware. Do not use base64/JSON for every PCM frame unless the verified transport requires it.

Proposed initial queue cap: **2 seconds per active track**, plus a strict byte cap derived from the negotiated format. This is a bounded volatile in-flight queue, not a durable retry buffer. Flush only inside a still-authorized live interval; never upload pre-wake conversation or replay an entire meeting after connectivity returns. On overflow, mark an explicit timestamped gap and stop/resynchronize as the protocol requires. A queue cap is not an endpointing setting.

Bound reconnects with jitter, total deadline and a new upstream-attempt identity. Reconcile segment overlaps when a small authorized in-memory tail must be replayed; never double-count text or hide the separately billed attempt. Do not assume the provider resumes a closed stream at a previous offset. If seamless continuation is unsupported, show the interruption and create a new epoch.

VAD and endpointing must not delete negations or final syllables. Preserve audio timing when handling silence and use documented keep-alive behavior only while capture is actively authorized. A long-lived silence stream may still incur cost; do not send fake silent audio to keep the cloud warm before wake. Meter unneeded open time and eliminate it rather than obscuring it.

### 9.9 Partial, utterance-final and stream-final are different

Preserve and extend current end-of-stream checks. An utterance marked `is_final` or `speech_final` is not proof the entire stream was finalized. A socket close is not proof all final words were received. Normalize the selected provider's actual terminal semantics and errors; test truncated tails, delayed terminal metadata, malformed frames, duplicate finals, cumulative offsets and language changes. [R51]

Use stable segment identity from capture generation, track, upstream epoch and actual source time/revision, not a fresh random segment ID for every interim. Partial revisions replace the relevant text, never append duplicate words. Keep command finality separate from a meeting pause. Do not execute “close Chrome” while a subsequent stable “no, keep it open” is still part of the same incomplete instruction.

Normal Stop may request a bounded provider tail flush for already-authorized audio. Stop-all, revoked consent, sign-out or a privacy failure revokes command authority immediately and prevents new audio submission; do not wait for a graceful flush to enforce safety. Any permitted final response may update only its still-valid transcript scope and cannot revive execution. Release sockets, callbacks, native handles and queues on every path.

### 9.10 Model configuration and fallbacks

Freeze separate meeting and command option profiles for the same default provider. Validate language versus auto-detection behavior, multilingual/code-switch support, diarization versus known physical channel, normalization/formatting and endpointing against the **Cloudflare-hosted** model's accepted schema. Do not assume every direct-Deepgram option works identically through Workers AI, or “fix” a rejected option by ignoring the response. Custom vocabulary is sensitive content, not log-safe metadata.

Qualify one primary Cloudflare live model/config before adding alternatives. A different Cloudflare-hosted model may be assessed for a measurable latency/quality benefit, but automatic selection requires a reviewed allowlist, supported-language matrix, privacy evidence, budgets and semantic parity. No hidden `latest`, arbitrary model ID, external-provider failover, duplicate shadow transcription or race of several providers in normal use.

Fallback defaults are **off**. If Cloudflare is unavailable, keep typed Ask/manual control available and show cloud-speech unavailable. A preinstalled local pack is offered only if it is allowed, still qualified and explicitly selected or covered by a previously accepted exact fallback policy. Selecting Local-only prohibits cloud egress of speech, including on crash, unsupported language or resource pressure. No error should silently download a model or switch privacy modes.

### 9.11 Measured speech performance and efficiency gates

In addition to section 7, record these proposed gates on the actual profile and native test devices. Targets are qualification goals, not vendor SLAs or current measurements.

| Measurement | Proposed target / required behavior |
|---|---|
| Authorized request → upstream ready, warm connection path | p95 ≤800 ms on the documented healthy network; report cold path and failures separately. |
| Speech interval end → displayed partial for that interval | p95 ≤700 ms, without degrading the quality gates or flooding the UI with unstable revisions. |
| End of complete command → stable final/intent input | p95 ≤1,000 ms on the qualified healthy profile; endpointing and model-processing durations reported separately. |
| Local capture cancellation / authority revocation | p95 ≤100 ms; cloud close latency never extends permission to act. |
| Worker processing overhead excluding network/inference | Goal p95 ≤50 ms per control operation, measured without storing content. |
| Application in-flight queue | Never exceeds negotiated 2-second/byte limits; overflow is visible, not durable audio buffering. |
| Long meeting soak | At least 2 hours on each primary native platform/profile; bounded memory, orderly segment offsets and no abandoned sockets/helpers. |
| Default startup/local models | No optional weights downloaded, model process launched, accelerator allocated or hidden background inference started. |
| Microphone-only command | Exactly the requested track(s); no redundant cloud STT path for system audio or animation. |

Report p50/p95/p99, warm/cold, sample count, network conditions and failure denominator. Optimize capture/serialization/connection/renderer overhead before relaxing fidelity, privacy or authorizations. Avoid per-partial Jev calls; submit when the candidate state meaningfully changes or stabilizes. Keep audio processing, network callbacks, caption updates and animation from competing on the main UI thread.

### 9.12 End-to-end acceptance, not a connector smoke check

The required live path is: install the identified lean application → sign in/enroll → fetch policy → confirm Cloudflare default/privacy readiness → grant capture at a real gesture → wake → see the existing orb/beam and live caption → obtain a real Cloudflare transcript → resolve a qualified Jev or Laya decision → execute and verify an authorized action → close the speech session → reconcile metadata in the actual deployed portal.

Prove that path on Windows and native Mac, with Mac public-signing status kept separate. Also prove a consented two-track meeting, multilingual material, normal stop/tail, hardware switch, offline interruption, rejected capture, revoked device, local-only operation and a profile with no optional model files. Run content-sentinel tests over all controlled storage/log/export paths as section 16.6 requires. A successful dev-mode request or mock portal row is not a substitute.

### 9.13 Faithful long meetings without an unbounded transcript archive

Define separate bounded working memory for PCM transport, live transcript display and summary construction. The two-second transport queue is not permission to retain a two-hour raw audio copy, nor does clearing it prove transcript memory is bounded. Use a finite segment/revision window and incremental structured notes linked to source intervals; persist only policy-permitted summaries/actions in the approved knowledge location. Flush or compact at semantic boundaries without silently turning approximate notes into verbatim evidence. No raw transcript content may enter a Cloudflare workflow checkpoint, durable exception payload or telemetry queue.

When memory pressure or a crash removes unsaved transient material, expose the affected interval and recovery limit. Do not rebuild missing speech from an LLM's plausible guess, and do not secretly journal everything to claim perfect recovery. Authorized published summaries survive according to their storage policy; unsaved speech has a deliberately different recovery guarantee. Consent withdrawal and confidential-meeting controls apply to pending derived notes too.

Score critical errors independently from ordinary WER: negation reversal, wrong amount/date/name/owner, fabricated sentence and wrong command target. Publish error, abstention, clarification and completion denominators together. Blanket “uncertain” labels or refusals cannot produce a fake accuracy pass. Include Québec French, accented English, Spanish, Brazilian Portuguese and relevant switching in their actual advertised scope; an unqualified slice remains unavailable or clearly limited. A human-reviewed gold corpus is a permissioned test asset, not a mandate to retain production recordings.

## 10. Jev and Laya decision service

### 10.1 Finish the real path

Locate the actual client, gateway, secret vault, entitlement and runtime call sites. The prior audit found a client path whose successful decision was discarded. Replace that no-op with a validated, useful decision, while keeping deterministic policy authoritative. A UI saying “Jev active” is not sufficient: provide one real trace from a microphone command through a Jev decision to an applied, verified action and its usage record. [R02]

Use a dedicated decision-provider interface, distinct from chat completion and speech providers. Normalize only genuinely comparable fields. Preserve backend/model/checkpoint version, actual decision semantics, candidate set, optional confidence, request/attempt ID, latency and available usage provenance. An unsupported field stays unavailable; never fabricate a probability to make providers look interchangeable.

### 10.2 Candidate-bound request and response

Build a small observed state from the current authorized task, target snapshot and allowed capabilities. Filter by platform, permissions, risk policy and available adapters before inference. Jev/Laya selects from candidate IDs and typed questions; it must not produce a raw shell command, selector, executable JavaScript or unrestricted filesystem path. Free text comes from the user's exact spans or an approved generative helper, then independent schema/content validation.

An example logical contract—not a promise of either vendor's exact wire format—is:

```json
{
  "schemaVersion": "metis.decision.v1",
  "operationId": "op_example",
  "captureGeneration": 12,
  "snapshotId": "snapshot_example",
  "intent": "open the meeting notes",
  "candidateIds": ["notes.app", "metis.latest-summary"],
  "questions": ["operation", "target", "needs_clarification"],
  "deadlineMs": 800
}
```

The server derives tenant/user/seat authority from authentication, never from an untrusted JSON identity field. The result references only permitted candidates and the exact snapshot. Apply it only if generation, target and policy are current. Keep probabilities as decision evidence, not authorization. Clarify when materially ambiguous; an index pointing at a stale DOM node is not a valid target.

### 10.3 Laya is a required alternative, not a claim of identical performance

Review `mysticalsin/laya`, its provenance/upstream, code and checkpoint licenses, dependency versions, model loader, language router, context and option-count limits. The inspected project is a Python/Torch typed-decision runtime. Host it in an approved service environment behind Operator by default; do not add Python/Torch and its weights to every desktop core. Select supported checkpoints and size residency/preloading deliberately. [R22]

Benchmark the same command and Intelligence decision sets against Jev. Test multilingual/script routing, tiny utterances, many candidates, uncertainty, invalid output, overload, cancellation, cold starts, model upgrades and unavailable hardware. Calibrate thresholds per provider/task. Do not silently truncate away approval-sensitive state to fit a shorter Laya context; narrow the candidate task or clarify. A short-context model is not a drop-in replacement for a large-history compaction request.

A Laya-only selection must never silently fall back to a commercial vendor. With no Jev key, use Laya only if the service is configured, entitled, available and qualified for that task; otherwise keep the deterministic safe path or show an actionable unavailable state. No enabled Jev key does not mean all other infrastructure is magically present.

“No Jev API fee” is an honest potential cost property; “everything is free” is not. Account for CPU/GPU serving, storage, operations, speech recognition and any generative helper independently. The portal must show the billing basis and unknown infrastructure allocation rather than default all Laya usage to zero total cost.

### 10.4 Portal provider controls

Implement tested settings for decision backend, endpoint/service identity, model/checkpoint version, allowed tasks, language coverage, health, region, timeouts, rate/budget limits and fallback policy. Secrets are masked and written to the existing server vault with audit events; a test button uses a synthetic input and exposes readiness, not key contents. Never make the desktop receive the vendor secret.

Provide an advanced per-operation receipt with provider/model, outcome, elapsed timing, permitted target evidence and usage completeness. Keep this separate from the minimal user-facing orb bar. For the showcase recording, prove at least one real Jev command and one real Laya command through the same contracts. Shadow comparison, when explicitly permitted, has no authority to execute a second action and has separately recorded consumption.

### 10.5 Efficient browser pattern

Adapt the strongest idea from `jev-ultrafast`: one fresh structured observation, compatible operation/target questions in a single decision cycle, a small text helper only when necessary, and deterministic freshness/occlusion checks before input. Its published demo is not a universal Métis latency or reliability guarantee. [R23]

Use one explicitly authorized browser session. Prefer existing supported browser integration without bundling another Chromium. If a controlled browser is necessary, make its profile, memory, disk, consent and lifecycle explicit. Do not attach to open debugging ports, scan profiles, export cookies or keep remote debugging exposed merely because a demo does so.

### 10.6 One key, independent capabilities, compatible decisions

Restore the original fleet requirement explicitly: one authorized administrator configures a server-stored Jev credential and two or more independently authenticated eligible installations use it without receiving it. Separate capability switches for **desktop decision assistance** and **Intelligence assessments**. Disabling Jev must not disable deterministic actions, ordinary speech, generative Ask or confirmed business facts. A revoked seat cannot use stale capability metadata to spend the shared key. [R72]

Test replacement-key validation, atomic rotation, concurrent requests, failed replacement preservation, sanitized upstream errors, service disable/revoke and cache invalidation. Old clients must tolerate the negotiated additive capability schema; a portal update cannot add missing executable code to an old binary. Deployment uses the normal signed client update where needed.

Jev's questions are evaluated independently over one state. An operation and target selected in different heads may be incompatible: validate the **pair and arguments** against a prevalidated candidate relationship, or choose among complete allowed candidate actions. The same rule applies to Laya. Confidence is not permission and must not be fabricated for fields the backend does not expose. [R76, R77]

Laya bring-up is independent of possessing a Jev key. It depends on the common decision contract and its own authorized service/checkpoint, not a successful live Jev request. Both real demonstrations remain required for the promised dual-provider product. A deterministic route can operate without either model where the exact requested action is already unambiguous; do not call a model purely to illuminate the orb.

## 11. Native macOS and Apple intelligence

### 11.1 Native work is mandatory even while signing is pending

Inspect the existing SwiftUI project and implement the real product using native windowing, accessibility, audio, storage/keychain and lifecycle APIs. Use signed/qualified helper boundaries where needed, not an embedded arbitrary command bridge. Preserve established IDs where technically safe; if a separate native bundle ID is necessary, explain TCC, updater and data migration implications rather than breaking them silently.

Maintain shared capability/telemetry contract fixtures between Swift and TypeScript. Prove the same open/close/notes/camera/meeting journeys with native APIs and visible OS permission behavior. Native UI packages must be compiled through a real Xcode target on Mac. Cross-compilation, static analysis or a browser rendering does not establish native functionality.

The Mac deliverable is a native app with a tested installer/migration plan. Public Developer ID DMG signing/notarization remains explicitly BLOCKED until legitimate credentials and enrollment are available. Development signing/ad-hoc local QA can be used only under the authorized development policy and must be labelled as such.

### 11.2 PCC: a real developer capability with a distribution condition

Apple's current eligibility page provides developer PCC access for accounts enrolled in the App Store Small Business Program, below its first-time download threshold, and granted the managed PCC entitlement. The documented public distribution path is the App Store, with TestFlight/ad-hoc testing. It does **not** establish public Developer ID DMG eligibility. Verify the actual shipping account and Apple approval; do not assume Tony's or Mantu's eligibility. [R10]

Therefore create an Apple capability/distribution matrix, not a universal “PCC default” boolean:

| Native configuration | Required behavior |
|---|---|
| Eligible device/OS/account, entitled App Store distribution, permitted task and available quota | Prefer supported PCC for the selected native generative workload after readiness checks. |
| Eligible supported on-device Apple model and approved local-inference profile | Use the OS model for suitable work without bundling separate application weights; record the actual route. |
| Public Developer ID DMG with no documented PCC distribution authorization | Do not advertise or depend on PCC. Use approved on-device or Operator routes, or report unavailable. Obtain written/current platform authorization before changing this. |
| Apple Intelligence disabled, unsupported device/OS/region/language, entitlement absent or model not ready | Clear actionable readiness state; no fake “Apple connected.” |
| PCC quota reached, server unavailable or policy denies the route | Respect the supported SDK error and user quota; offer only permitted alternatives with disclosed cost/data handling. |
| Windows or a server trying to use a Mac as a PCC relay | Not a supported Métis route. No quota/account/entitlement circumvention. |

Apple's Foundation Models material describes native PCC sessions and quota handling. Use the exact available SDK symbols and availability checks in the chosen Xcode/OS versions; verify whether any API remains preview/beta and qualify against the shipping OS. Do not freeze guessed context sizes, quotas or undocumented endpoints into the architecture. [R11, R12]

PCC is not speech recognition, wake detection, an app-control permission, or a service that authorizes tool calls. Its generation must still feed a validated context/output contract. Jev/Laya decision tasks remain their own configured capability; do not replace them invisibly merely to claim free Apple inference.

### 11.3 Apple cost and portal measurement

Eligible PCC use may have no cloud API charge to the developer under Apple's documented program. That does not mean unlimited availability or zero total operating cost. Record first-party native events for requests, completion/failure, latency, route/model identification exposed by the SDK, quota status exposed by the SDK and token quantities **only if actually available for that operation**. [R10–R12]

Classify these as device-reported/SDK-observed metadata, not an independently reconciled Apple invoice. Unknown input/output counts stay null; do not reverse-engineer private APIs or estimate numbers and label them exact. Use separate fields for `vendor_api_cost`, `allocated_infrastructure_cost`, `measurement_basis` and `completeness`. Zero eligible API charge is not the same as zero tokens or no work.

PCC calls follow Apple's native secure path. Operator receives minimal permitted telemetry, not prompts/audio just to make a dashboard. The portal cannot claim visibility into all of a user's Apple Intelligence or iCloud activity. Respect per-user quota semantics and no task routing around a quota or account restriction.

### 11.4 Native speech and asset management

Cloudflare remains the default native **speech** route. Apple on-device speech is an optional Settings choice; native PCC/on-device **generation** is independent and does not silently change the speech engine.

Evaluate Apple's supported Speech framework and OS-managed model assets for an explicitly enabled native on-device profile. Apple documents asset-installation requests that consolidate model downloads. Use those supported lifecycle APIs where applicable rather than copying OS model files into R2 or the app. [R13]

Do not assume those models cover every required language or match the cloud benchmark; measure them. A managed cloud-only deployment must not silently enable on-device speech. Clarify whether the chosen policy exception applies to wake detection, on-device generation, speech, or PCC individually. “Native” alone does not settle that privacy decision.

## 12. Operator, central skills, identity and administration

### 12.1 Repair the deployed chain, not just its appearance

Trace an authorized synthetic operation from a packaged client through authentication, ingestion, provider completion, storage, aggregation, frontend query and export. Record desktop version, Worker version, database schema, frontend bundle and timestamp window. A beautiful portal screenshot using fixtures is not end-to-end proof.

Keep the existing Operator infrastructure where it can meet the requirements. Use independent runtime readouts for deployment/version mismatch, failed auth, stale heartbeat, ingestion backlog, missing metering, unapplied migrations, outdated frontend bundle and incomplete provider configuration. An empty UI must distinguish “no activity,” “not authorized,” “not connected,” “still loading” and “data could not be loaded.”

### 12.2 Required portal surfaces

| Surface | Required functionality |
|---|---|
| Overview | Distinct verified people, devices, meaningful active use, requests, actions, reported consumption, known cost, failure rate and freshness. Define each metric visibly. |
| People | Verified identity, organization/access scope, associated installations, session history, versions, first/last activity and attributable usage. No guessed identity from hostname/email text. |
| Devices | OS/architecture/app version, enrollment, approvals, policy revision, last heartbeat, last meaningful action, capture capability readiness and approved component inventory. |
| Usage and cost | Time-series and drill-down by authorized person/device/feature/backend/model/funding path; input/output/cache/decision/audio quantities where reported; consistent export. |
| Speech | Effective Cloudflare model/route, policy/privacy-readiness revision, track counts, processed/sent duration, speech latency, gaps, disconnects, safe failure reasons and usage completeness. Never transcripts or custom vocabulary. |
| Local models | Coarse authorized device class, compatible/installed/selected/loaded states, pack versions and integrity/benchmark outcome; no serial numbers or raw hardware fingerprint by default. |
| Decisions | Jev/Laya readiness/version, real route selection, error/latency, calibration qualification and explicit fallback policy. |
| Native Apple | Readiness/entitlement route state, SDK-reported quota/metadata where available, not an invented PCC billing dashboard. |
| Installation and releases | Core/component versions, expected vs installed manifest, integrity failures, update outcome, signed artifact provenance and support channel. |
| Data health | Unresolved identities, missing/partial usage, duplicate/rejected events, queue age, pricing gaps, delayed records and pipeline incidents. |
| Administration/audit | Role-scoped device approval, revocation, provider/policy changes, budgets, exports and accountable operator audit records. |

Use clear date-range semantics including the user's `range=24h`: define rolling versus calendar intervals and time zone. Server aggregates cover the full authorized interval. UI table pages, detail caps or chart sampling must not truncate KPI totals. Charts and exports share the same filtered aggregation definitions and show coverage/unknown portions.

### 12.3 Identity and permissions

Separate authenticated principal, organization scope, installation/device, seat/license and session. Use verified stable identity claims where available; email is display/contact data, not the only durable identity. Attribute each event to its authenticated identity at event time. A later login on a shared computer cannot reassign previous spending to a new person. Handle sign-out, reinstallation, renamed device, identity merge, revocation and unresolved identity explicitly.

Connectivity means recent heartbeat. Activity means a defined meaningful interaction, not merely an app running in the background. Distinct people, devices, sessions and licensed seats must not be aliases for each other.

Implement the appropriate existing role model or a minimal extension: platform administrator, scoped organizational administrator, support viewer, billing/usage viewer and end-user own-data view where needed. Enforce access in queries, exports and caches, not only disabled buttons. If Métis is an internal single-organization product, do not add an unrequested multi-tenant SaaS rewrite; nevertheless document and test the actual organizational boundaries before widening deployment.

### 12.4 Central skills: authored once, safely used across Métis

The Operator portal is the administration and skill control plane. The normal desktop/native/Teams client selects an available skill and renders its result; it does not download arbitrary skill code or run a second local agent platform. Preserve optional local model functionality as a separate explicit feature, not a fallback for an unavailable cloud skill.

The repository already describes signed skill-pack distribution and manifest polling. Reuse the relevant IDs, ownership, review and version structures, but audit what is actually executed and where. Serving a signed pack to a client is not proof of server-side skill execution. Migrate deliberately; do not install a competing registry simply because a reference repository offers one. [R56]

Define a skill as a versioned, declarative capability: instructions, typed input/output, permitted context sources, bounded tool references, risk/approval rules, approved model/route policy and measurable outcome. Distinguish this from an executable package, a local model, a saved prompt, a Dust agent and a knowledge record. A skill can use a Dust agent through an authorized integration, but calling Dust does not bypass Métis permissions or retention.

### 12.5 Skill definition and publishing lifecycle

Each immutable skill version needs stable ID, version/digest, title, purpose, owner, author/reviewer, languages, input/output schema, audience/entitlements, retrieval scope, evidence requirements, tool allowlist, provider/privacy class, cost/time/context limits, minimum client/protocol version, synthetic test set, dependency versions and migration/deprecation rules. Keep sensitive credentials out of the definition; use approved server secret references.

Publishing follows **Draft → Validate → Synthetic evaluation → Review → Canary → Published → Deprecated/Revoked**. Updating an existing skill creates a new version; it never silently changes the bytes behind an old version. A stable channel points atomically to the approved version. Pin one version, tool registry revision and policy snapshot per run; a mid-run update must not change the instruction set beneath an ongoing action.

A security revocation takes effect at the execution boundary, including queued/active runs, regardless of the pinned skill version. Ordinary upgrades affect subsequent runs. Rollback selects a permitted previously qualified version; it cannot restore revoked permissions or a blocked vulnerability. Maintain a visible diff, test results, publishing actor and reason for every promotion.

Import is a reviewed authoring workflow. Accept safe supported declarative formats; quarantine untrusted archives. No remote-code execution, npm install, arbitrary shell script, untrusted MCP endpoint, undeclared network destination or newly privileged tool follows from uploading a ZIP. Validate size, paths, dependency origin and allowed asset types. The production runtime only calls already reviewed bounded tool adapters.

### 12.6 Runtime service, bounded context and action authority

Logical run flow:

```text
Authenticated desktop / native Mac / Teams / approved Dust caller
  → select an entitled published skill and pin its version
  → validate typed inputs and intended output audience
  → retrieve an authorized, versioned Mantu Intelligence evidence packet
  → run the approved server-side model/tool workflow within budgets
  → validate output, citations and target-specific approvals
  → commit only allowed actions or save the approved output
  → return result and metadata-only run receipt
```

Operator may orchestrate network-bound inference and metadata. Use an existing approved isolated service for tasks that genuinely need heavier rendering or runtimes beyond the Worker envelope. Do not pretend all Python/native workloads fit a standard Worker, and do not provision paid infrastructure without authorization. Keep execution near the authorized knowledge location when this reduces unnecessary content transit.

No skill gets more access than the authenticated caller, the skill's own allowed scope and the target audience permit. Apply the intersection in trusted code before retrieval and before each side effect. User text, retrieved instructions or the model cannot widen tools, destinations, budgets or approvals. Large prompt context is not permission to collect every meeting.

Support cancellation, deadlines, bounded steps, backpressure, retry classification and reconciliation. Store durable resumable references/status only where content storage is prohibited; fetch content just in time from the approved knowledge location. If resumable execution needs a retained output/checkpoint, store it only in the authorized knowledge environment under its actual retention/ACL policy. Do not persist a hidden prompt/response archive in Operator for debugging.

A skill result is not a desktop control command. Only the existing trusted capability executor may perform OS actions, with local user/session authority and exact target verification. A remote Teams or Dust run cannot silently operate a sleeping user's laptop or convert meeting speech into an OS command.

### 12.7 Keep authored software separate from private run content

Revision 2 prohibited durable inference prompts/results in Cloudflare. That remains true for **user/session content**. A reviewed skill template, schema, release manifest or public interface asset is authored software/configuration, not a captured user prompt. Such artifacts may live in the approved control plane if their classification permits it. An uploaded customer document, sample meeting, secret, confidential playbook or populated prompt must not be relabelled “template” to evade content policy.

Confidential skill knowledge stays in the selected approved knowledge repository with scoped references. The server assembles it transiently for the authorized run. Operator stores the skill/version identifiers and approved control metadata, not customer-specific example conversations or generated outputs. Evaluation fixtures are synthetic and explicitly classified; production feedback does not automatically become a training dataset.

Local clients receive only the small catalog/availability metadata and permitted result/context needed for display. They do not receive corporate provider keys, unrestricted templates for bypass, or executable downloads. Clearly label cloud skills unavailable offline; do not secretly substitute a local prompt or an older revoked cached skill.

### 12.8 User catalog and selection

Expose a small curated set of useful outcomes—Prepare my meeting, Summarize and extract actions, Where do we stand?, Draft follow-up, Review commitments—within Knowledge & skills and relevant contextual actions. Show only entitled/ready skills and explain missing prerequisites in plain language. Favorite/recent skills are conveniences, not a second permission system.

Natural language can suggest an appropriate skill from the approved catalog. A suggestion is not permission to run a costly or side-effecting workflow. Deterministic eligibility filters run before model ranking; confidence cannot override restrictions. Maintain a bounded context budget and avoid sending the entire catalog on every command. Context-aware selection must disclose when a source/meeting is ambiguous.

Use progressive disclosure: normal users see title, purpose, relevant source/audience and simple inputs; admins see schemas, models, budgets and tool governance. A new published skill should become available without reinstalling Métis, but only after catalog refresh and validation. Pin/freshness/compatibility errors must be honest rather than hidden under a permanent Ready badge.

### 12.9 Operator skill and knowledge operations

Extend Operator with Skills, Knowledge health and Meetings operations alongside existing Users, Devices, Usage and Providers. Avoid duplicating dashboards that calculate different totals. Each page uses the same scoped definitions, date range and completeness signals.

Skill detail shows versions, audience, owner, evaluation status, canary/promotion, live runs, safe error categories, latency and attributable consumption. Knowledge health shows projection lag, unresolved identity, access failures and pending/conflicted mutations using approved metadata; record names/excerpts appear only through a separate authorized content view, never blanket administrative analytics.

Live means an explicitly defined open connection, executing operation or active capture—not evidence a human is productively working. Distinct people, connected devices, live meeting bots, skill runs and billable attempts remain separate metrics. Do not infer employee productivity, emotion, performance or sentiment from meeting attendance or token spend.

Meter every real provider attempt once, linked to the logical run and pinned skill version. Speech minutes, Jev/Laya decisions, generative tokens and hosting allocation remain different units. A Dust charge observed only as workspace aggregate cannot be fabricated as an exact per-user API charge; preserve basis and unavailable detail. Join/subscription/service-control calls are not generative tokens. A framework trace is not the accounting ledger.

### 12.10 Required skill proofs

Publish a synthetic test skill in authorized staging, use it from Windows and native Mac, update its instructions to a new version, and demonstrate that the next run uses that version without an app reinstall while an already started run retains its pinned version. Revoke it and verify that cached catalogs and queued runs cannot bypass the block. Roll back to a still-allowed version and verify the output/version receipt.

Use a real Mantu Intelligence retrieval under two principals with different permissions; prove the skill cannot disclose the restricted source to the second user or to a wider meeting audience. Exercise malformed inputs, injection-bearing documents, secret-bearing errors, tool denial, cancellation after partial execution and incomplete usage settlement. Connect the actual gateway and portal—not a fixture-only run list.

## 13. Usage, cost, quotas and accounting correctness

### 13.1 Ledger semantics

A user operation may have zero, one or many provider attempts. Assign a stable server-trusted attempt identity and record each independently chargeable attempt once. Retransmitting the same metering event is not another charge. Reattempting a genuinely new model call may be another charge even if the user sees one answer.

Prefer server-authoritative usage from the gateway/provider. Merge client feature, timing and interaction metadata without erasing that usage. Preserve device ownership and reject cross-principal overwrites. Late, incomplete or less-authoritative data cannot downgrade known authoritative quantities; corrections must have explicit provenance and an auditable reconciliation rule.

Use provider-specific normalized fields, not a universal raw total assembled by guesswork: input, output, cache read/write/uncached, reasoning or other reported categories, requests/decisions/questions, audio seconds, compute time and units billed. Model provider cache inclusion/overlap rules correctly. Preserve literal zero versus missing, estimated, partial and provider-reported. Do not count cumulative stream snapshots as deltas.

### 13.2 Data record design

Extend the existing store minimally or add purpose-built append/reconcile tables with a migration, indexes and compatibility policy. Required logical fields include authenticated scope; user/device/session; operation/attempt/step; provider/model/checkpoint and pricing revision; start/completion timestamps; status; known quantities and units; authoritative source; reconciliation/completeness state; route/funding path; and safe error classification. Do not add raw prompts, audio, transcript text, custom vocabulary, filenames, screenshots, shell history or answer bodies merely for accounting. Content hashes/embeddings and free-form previews are not acceptable substitutes for content-free metering.

Record tariff currency and effective dates, use decimal/integer-safe money arithmetic, and distinguish vendor cost from organizational allocation/estimated infrastructure cost. A model alias must resolve to the actual billed/versioned model where the service exposes it. Unknown tariffs produce unknown cost, not zero. Recompute a tariff correction transparently without changing the underlying measured quantities.

### 13.3 Streaming, durability and budgets

Consume usage-bearing provider events correctly, including initial nested usage, final usage-only events, cumulative counts, interruption and known billable failed attempts. Add contract fixtures for each enabled provider. A successful text stream with a failed metering write must surface accounting-health degradation and durable reconciliation; it must not silently erase spending.

Use an existing outbox/queue or minimal durable repair mechanism appropriate to the Worker/database environment. Do not create unbounded retries or duplicate charges after ambiguous completion. Record event-time and ingestion-time separately; cap queue retention and protect permitted metadata at rest.

Check identity, entitlement, rate limits and cost-policy eligibility before starting billable work. Use concurrency-safe budget reservation/settlement where hard limits are promised; a periodically updated dashboard is not a hard spending cap. When authoritative accounting or reservation is unavailable, follow the approved safety policy for paid work instead of allowing unbounded spend. Local low-risk actions can remain available without a billing pipeline.

### 13.4 Speech quantities and metadata-only durability

Speech is not naturally billed as generative output tokens. Record declared model/version and route, transport, active/requested tracks, source duration sent, provider-processed duration when exposed, retries, safe timing/error fields, tariff revision and quantity provenance. Distinguish wall-clock meeting duration from the sum of processed track durations. Reconcile the selected tariff's treatment of channels, silence, reconnects and rounding; do not invent universal multipliers or overwrite missing upstream quantities with client estimates labelled authoritative. [R16]

The exact numeric usage ledger must remain durable when content logging is disabled. Capture provider completion metadata in the authorized relay before cleanup where available, with periodic **metadata-only** progress records for long sessions and idempotent attempt settlement. Do not keep a recording or full provider message just to recover an invoice. Separate an unsent frame, a replayed frame and billable processing; known billable duplicate attempts remain measurable even when their words are deduplicated for the user.

An accounting outbox/queue may hold allowlisted metadata only. Enforce projection before enqueue, database write, alert, diagnostic export and retry; schema validation must reject unexpected strings carrying user content. For unknown or unavailable quantities, record null plus a reason. Keep operational retention finite and approved (proposed default: 30 days for raw operational events, 90 days for content-free daily aggregates, subject to the actual accounting/privacy policy); identity and security-audit retention are independently documented. This is not a claim that Cloudflare retains no service metadata.

### 13.5 Mandatory accounting test cases

| Test | Required result |
|---|---|
| 2,001 one-token attempts in a selected interval | KPI =2,001, regardless of a 2,000-row detail limit. |
| Server records 120 input /30 output; later same-device client omits usage | Authoritative 120/30 survives; client metadata may merge. |
| A different device reuses another attempt ID | No ownership or usage overwrite. |
| Provider cumulative output events are 1,15,30 | Final output=30, not46. |
| Two different chargeable attempts serve one operation | Two attempts, one user operation; each retry delivery deduplicated. |
| Interrupted response has partial reported consumption | Retain known consumption and partial/unknown balance; not automatically zero. |
| Input/output known but cache breakdown missing | Display known totals plus unknown breakdown; no misleading empty consumption chart. |
| Laya returns decisions without generative output tokens | Record real decision/compute quantities; no invented completion-token bill. |
| Eligible Apple API cost is zero but SDK token count unavailable | Cost basis and unavailable token quantity remain distinct. |
| Shared device changes signed-in user | Historical attribution remains bound to the correct original principal/session. |
| Two concurrent requests approach a hard budget | Atomic policy enforcement prevents overspend according to the documented reservation rule. |
| Two tracks span one meeting plus an upstream reconnect | One meeting, appropriate distinct track/attempt quantities; current tariff rules determine cost, not a guessed duration. |
| Gateway content logging is disabled | The authorized metadata ledger and full-window portal totals still reconcile; missing data is not painted as zero. |
| A speech error includes transcript/vocabulary in its raw payload | Only a safe error code is persisted; content is excluded from logs, queues, traces and exports. |
| Provider usage is unavailable on a cancelled stream | Preserve known sent/processed quantities and distinguish estimates; no false exact cost. |

Maintain a reconciliation view showing known, estimated, partial and unavailable coverage. The end-to-end portal gate requires actual backend events and database aggregates from a permitted test environment, not only a local SQLite illustration or hand-constructed fixture.

## 14. Lightweight installation, device-aware optional models and Cloudflare delivery

### 14.1 The concrete size opportunity

The inspected runtime manifest lists 803,739,695 bytes of speech/runtime assets. A separate local-model manifest adds 763,771,056 bytes including its license. Together they total 1,567,510,751 bytes. The largest named contributors are the Parakeet core files and the Qwen model plus vision projector. This is uncompressed declared inventory; it neither proves all files occur once in a specific installer nor predicts their compressed saving. [R03, R04]

| Declared source inventory | Bytes | Proposed cloud-first treatment |
|---|---:|---|
| Parakeet core encoder/decoder/joiner | 670,384,833 | Optional approved offline speech component, not default cloud core. |
| Qwen model | 558,772,480 | Optional local-generation pack, never required just to launch cloud Ask. |
| Qwen vision projector | 204,987,232 | Separate opt-in capability; do not load/download for text-only use. |
| Remaining speech/runtime manifest assets | 133,354,862 | Inspect actual dependencies and profile requirements; remove only what the selected core does not need. |
| Local-model license | 11,344 | Retain applicable notices with every distributed component. |

First produce a machine-readable size report for each actual build: compressed installer bytes, unpacked app resources, selected architecture, duplicated dependency trees, native binaries, model weights, videos, caches, peak extraction space, temporary download space and steady installed size. Report process-tree memory separately; an installer shrinking does not establish a lighter running app.

### 14.2 Recommended distribution model

**Primary recommendation: a genuinely small signed core plus optional verified components.** Keep the current cross-platform foundation and avoid a framework rewrite purely for size. The core includes the app, required OS helpers, compact wake functionality if enabled, essential UI/assets, profile/manifest verification and cloud adapters. It should be useful without a local generative model or full offline speech package.

Proposed measured goals: Windows compressed cloud-first core ≤250MB and steady core footprint ≤650MB; native Mac app distribution ≤100MB where feasible, excluding OS-managed models. Treat these as budgets to validate against native helpers and current framework overhead, not promised results. Quantify before/after on the same product profile and exact artifact. Explain any exception instead of hiding bytes in a cache.

Offer three explicit delivery choices:

| Delivery | Role | Important constraint |
|---|---|---|
| Signed compact full core installer | Default and managed-IT-friendly Windows download | No internet required merely to verify/install the core; cloud features naturally need authorized connectivity. |
| Optional signed NSIS web bootstrap | Small initial download that fetches the matching signed core payload | This changes transfer timing, not total bytes. Enable only after version-specific payload integrity/signature and update behavior are proven. |
| Full offline enterprise bundle | IT-approved deployments that need local speech/model capabilities | Include only selected supported packs/architecture; label its larger size and policy clearly. |

Electron-builder supports NSIS web installers, but their payload verification/update behavior must be reviewed for the pinned toolchain; newer documentation explicitly discusses security changes around web-installer update payloads. Do not bypass such protection to satisfy a “tiny installer” target. Preserve a standard signed core installer even if the bootstrap is offered. [R17, R18]

Wi-Fi is not a separate installation technology. It is a network transport. The product downloads needed content over an authorized connection, obeying metered/offline/network policy, whether Wi-Fi or Ethernet. Do not require a particular radio connection or assume every Wi-Fi network is free/unmetered.

### 14.3 Cloudflare responsibilities

Use R2 for reviewed immutable binary/data assets, not user transcripts. Use a Worker for authenticated manifests, release/channel policy and short-lived authorized download grants. Reuse Operator identity and entitlements where appropriate; ensure a new installation has a safe bootstrap/auth flow rather than demanding a vendor master key.

Keep manifest authorization and asset delivery separate. Public, identical signed application binaries may use a deliberate public distribution endpoint; private/entitled component metadata remains authorized. Scope caches so private manifests/grants do not leak between organizations. Presigned R2 URLs are bearer grants and use the S3 endpoint rather than automatically working with a custom domain; use the documented binding/Worker pattern when a custom domain is required. Redact query credentials from logs and support reports. [R19]

R2 stores files. It does not run Laya inference. Cloudflare Workers may coordinate the service, but do not assume Python/Torch GPU workloads fit a standard Worker. Select and budget the actual authorized Laya serving environment separately. Do not add a hosted GPU account without approval.

### 14.4 Signed component manifest and trust chain

Use a versioned, signed manifest with component ID/version, content digest, exact byte length, architecture/OS constraints, app-version compatibility, dependency IDs, capability/profile requirements, unpacked-size bound, license/notices, signing identity expectations for executable content, minimum safe version and release/channel revision. The trusted verification root belongs in the signed application or an authenticated approved root-update chain—not solely next to the downloadable manifest.

A checksum fetched from the same compromised location is not an independent trust root. Verify the manifest signature, authorization, freshness/rollback policy, component hash/length and executable signer before activation. Reject unknown algorithms/schema versions, malformed IDs, path traversal, absolute archive paths, symlinks outside the target, decompression bombs, duplicate conflicting entries and architecture mismatches.

Pin immutable content-addressed assets. Do not replace bytes behind a previously qualified version/hash. Support signing-key rotation through an explicit trusted chain. Keep private manifest/signing keys in the authorized release secret system. Separate legitimate rollback to a permitted known-good version from downgrade to a vulnerable version; define offline handling of an already installed trusted component without making verification a network-dependent launch trap.

### 14.5 Download and activation lifecycle

The trusted core—not the renderer—owns installation. Suggested lifecycle:

```text
Requested → policy/space/network check → authorized manifest
→ download to isolated temporary path → length/hash/signature verification
→ safe extraction → compatibility/self-test → atomic activation
→ usable capability → bounded cleanup/rollback inventory
```

Stream downloads to disk with bounded memory, cancellation and safe timeouts. Support resumable ranges only when server behavior and entity/digest consistency are verified; otherwise restart safely. Respect Retry-After and exponential backoff with jitter. Do not create a retry storm or silently restart a multi-GB transfer repeatedly. Check captive-portal HTML and content mismatch; never execute a response simply because HTTP status is200.

Estimate peak disk demand including download, extraction, old version and rollback copy. Handle low space before transfer where possible and during extraction safely. A crash, sleep, reboot or user cancel may leave a temporary file but must not advertise a partially activated capability. Limit stale partial retention and cleanup only app-owned files.

Downloadable executable helpers require legitimate platform signing and a constrained activation mechanism; do not mutate a signed Mac app bundle after installation. Prefer shipping critical privileged helpers inside the signed core. Models/data may live in a controlled application-support component store with verified manifests. Do not load arbitrary remote JavaScript/TypeScript, enable remote Node integration or install from an unreviewed npm/Git repository at runtime.

### 14.6 Onboarding uses the time, but never hides work

During the intro video, the installer can validate the core, authenticate policy, assess capabilities, obtain an approved manifest and download **selected necessary** components. The cloud-first profile should normally need only small required assets, not all historic speech/model bundles. Download optional offline packs only after explicit selection or a trusted IT policy.

Show exact stage, bytes, total where known, truthful indeterminate progress where unknown, ETA only when justified, and pause/retry controls. “Ready” means verification and self-test completed, not download started or the video ended. A long video is not permission to consume bandwidth silently. Slow networks must not leave users trapped on an endlessly looping cinematic screen.

Existing offline-package/runtime checks need a reviewed profile-aware redesign: test the required inventory for `cloud-first`, `offline-selected` and each supported native profile. Do not delete checks or turn errors into warnings simply because optional assets were removed. A selected offline capability missing its pack is a real failure; an unselected local model missing from a cloud-only installation is not.


### 14.7 Device-aware optional local models: Settings is the control plane

Expose two distinct capabilities through the simplified navigation: **Voice & meetings → Optional local speech** and **Advanced → Optional local generation**. A vision projector is an additional explicitly selected capability, not an invisible dependency of text generation. Both local sections are off by default. Cloudflare being unavailable is not consent to install, and installed model bytes are not consent to load or select a local engine.

The device's marketed model name is only a hint. Inspect actual OS/build, architecture, CPU instructions, physical and available memory, available disk/peak extraction space, supported GPU/NPU/Metal/runtime and driver, accelerator memory where exposed, power/thermal state and required language coverage. Prefer OS-supported APIs and existing probes. Do not infer usable GPU acceleration from vendor name, expose a unique fingerprint or upload raw serial numbers. Permission to evaluate hardware is not permission to benchmark indefinitely in the background.

Keep a compact, signed/reviewed model catalog with per-pack artifact/version/hash, platform/architecture, runtime/helper requirements, supported language/task set, measured peak memory, disk/download/temporary budget, execution providers actually tested, quality score and performance qualification version. Reuse existing manifests rather than maintaining a contradictory second list. A local speech pack is not a local LLM, and a Python/Torch-hosted Laya service does not belong in the desktop recommendation catalog by default.

Recommended selection is a two-stage process: static eligibility before download, then a bounded synthetic local self-test after the user requests and verifies that pack. Before any download, display all bytes and the possibility that a failed self-test will leave the model disabled with a removal option. Never fetch several candidate packs simply to decide which one is best.

Use a resource envelope instead of arbitrary RAM tiers: measured peak of the selected runtime/model + audio/rendering/core headroom + any explicitly concurrent model + OS/meeting-app reserve must fit the supported available memory and accelerator budget. Disk checks include the download, safe extraction, current version and rollback space. Insufficient or unknown accelerator data selects a measured supported CPU path only when its performance qualifies; otherwise explain incompatibility. Recheck the envelope before load, after driver/OS changes and under actual memory pressure.

For local real-time speech, a proposed initial gate is p95 real-time factor ≤0.7 on a representative permitted clip with the meeting UI active, plus the same language-specific quality gate used for its advertised profile. The precise reserve and test workload are per-model evidence, not a promise that every 8/16/32 GB computer behaves alike. Sustained heat, battery saver and a busy Teams/browser workload must be represented in device-class qualification.

### 14.8 Required local-model interface and lifecycle

| State | Required user-visible behavior |
|---|---|
| Off / not installed | Cloudflare remains selected; no pack bytes, background downloads or inference process. |
| Compatible / recommended | Explain why this pack fits, estimated resource use, supported languages and explicit download size. Recommendation never installs it. |
| Unsupported / insufficient resources | Specific safe reason and viable cloud/text path; no permissive “install anyway” under managed policy. |
| Downloading / paused / verifying | Actual progress, network/space checks, cancellation and signed-manifest verification. |
| Installed, not selected | Files are available but not loaded; no new capture or implicit selection. |
| Selected and ready | Effective policy shows Local; model is loaded on demand for the requested task and passes readiness. |
| Resource pressure / failed self-test | Pause/degrade within approved local settings or stop safely. Never switch to a cloud provider without exact prior authority. |
| Disabled / removed | Release processes/accelerator resources; remove only app-owned pack files on explicit uninstall; do not reinstall next launch. |

Settings must provide Install recommended, Review alternatives, Pause/resume/cancel, Select, Unload/disable and Remove where allowed. Show policy locks, disk cost, current selected route and whether an optional fallback was explicitly enabled. Do not present a cloud-only profile as locally capable. Update the help text to say that downloading a pack contacts the approved asset host even though subsequent local-only speech recognition does not send audio to the cloud.

Store machine-specific pack selection/capability assessment locally by default. Organization policy may define allowed packs/limits and receive a coarse inventory for support, not force one model onto all devices. Re-evaluate policy and model compatibility when a device or account changes; preserve user decisions and avoid unnecessary re-downloads of already-verified matching bytes.

Coordinate downloads/self-tests with capture: lower priority or pause them during an active meeting/command, enforce bounded concurrency, and never trade missing transcript frames for a faster optional installation. Load local models only when selected and needed; unload safely after the configured idle period unless an explicitly chosen warm-local mode is allowed and budgeted. The small wake detector remains a separate opt-in essential capability, not a justification to fetch a full offline ASR model.

## 15. Onboarding, upgrades, Teams and meeting participation

### 15.1 First-run journey

**Topology rule:** follow §32.6 and §34: improve the existing Métis scenes, not a replacement wizard. The welcome media is Tony-only or intentionally absent with a complete text fallback.

Use this order, adjusted only for verified technical dependencies: core integrity → user/organization identity → verified effective policy and Cloudflare content-privacy readiness → clear privacy choice and permissions at the relevant gesture → real Cloudflare speech readiness → optional device-qualified capabilities (not blocking cloud use) → a real first-success action → ready. The video is explanatory, not a security gate. Permit skipping/reducing purely educational motion while keeping required consent, identity and integrity gates intact.

Differentiate core ready, voice not yet permitted, cloud provider unavailable, optional offline pack downloading and public Mac release unavailable. Explain what works now. Offer text Ask and settings while optional setup continues. Never use a demonstration microphone or a simulated transcription to claim a real permission/service is ready.

For #196, reproduce the visible Act2 Next failure against the bundled renderer, trace validation/state/effect/clock ownership and implement the minimum proven correction. Keep required checks. For #197, preserve the focused `devEnv` packaged-renderer correction and its development-mode behavior. Test with the override unset, unreachable and a controlled reachable stale server; packaged overlay and decoder must not request it. Both must pass on the final Windows candidate and the Mac QA candidate separately.

### 15.2 Upgrade and migration

Test upgrades from the actual currently distributed builds, not only a clean profile. Record and reconcile the canonical customer feed: prior documentation and release destinations differed. Preserve `asktoto` internal identifiers unless a separately justified migration requires a change. Keep compatible encryption/key access, notes, app settings, licenses, entitlements, OneDrive paths and permission behavior.

Move from bundled to optional assets without redownloading verified matching bytes. Reuse matching app-owned assets through an approved migration where safe; do not crawl arbitrary user folders. Remove obsolete model copies only when not in use, backed by known inventory and a reversible cleanup decision. Do not delete a user's own model, transcript or custom dependency because its filename resembles an optional asset.

Use expand/contract migrations for portal/client compatibility. Support the defined preceding client version during rollout and rollback. A native Mac migration must explicitly import/locate existing data and key material through supported authorized paths; differing bundle/keychain identities are not a minor rename.

### 15.3 Resilience and support

Define recoverable failures with short user messages and safe diagnostic IDs: auth, policy, permission, unsupported OS/app, connection, model unavailable, quota, integrity, storage, sync conflict, timeout and unverified outcome. Diagnostic exports exclude keys, grant URLs, prompts, raw transcripts, screenshots and user file contents by default.

Define recovery objectives, backup ownership, retention and a restore drill for Operator and relevant configuration. Verify a real isolated staging environment rather than trusting a checked-in placeholder. A file-sync service is not by itself a tested backup strategy. Client crash recovery must obey summary-only policy; do not introduce raw audio/transcript journaling under the name “reliability.”

### 15.4 Teams integration: three different capabilities

Métis stays a standalone signed Windows application and a genuine native Mac application. Add a Teams app that reaches the same governed services; do not replace the desktop app with a tab or expose its privileged IPC to web content.

| Capability | Purpose | Does not establish |
|---|---|---|
| Personal Teams app/tab | Access own briefings, skills, meetings and settings with Entra-backed SSO. | Permission to read every meeting or control the desktop. |
| Meeting side panel / optional stage | Show participant-facing presence, current capture state, permitted shared notes and controls. | A real audio participant or permission to capture raw media. |
| Communications/meeting service | Join an eligible meeting as the visible Métis assistant and handle the approved media/transcript path. | Universal access, automatic lobby admission, or GDPR compliance. |

Separate app installation, identity, organizer authorization, capture consent/notice, media access and output sharing. A Teams tab does not itself grant call/media permissions. “Show Métis in Teams” and “Métis joins the meeting” must be tested separately. Provide a clear display name and supported in-meeting controls; no impersonation of the user or covert joining.

### 15.5 Entra ID across desktop, Teams, Dust and server actors

Use the existing Entra foundation and one verified identity mapping based on tenant and stable subject/object identity. Teams SSO can authenticate the tab user, but access to downstream Graph resources needs the appropriate delegated flow/permissions; the tab token is not a universal Graph credential. Validate actual issuer, audience, signature, lifetime, tenant and authorized client, and reject client-supplied identity fields as authority. [R59]

Use native desktop authorization-code/PKCE where applicable and a supported on-behalf-of flow on a trusted backend for delegated downstream operations. Separate application-only bot/automation permissions from the signed-in user's token and grant only the capabilities needed by that route. Managed identities/certificates and approved secret storage are preferable to copied long-lived keys. Never place tokens or join grants in ordinary deep links, browser storage accessible to arbitrary content, diagnostics or public client configuration.

Enforce tenant restrictions, conditional access and consent policy rather than turning off controls to make an integration test pass. Anonymous guests are not automatically Entra-authenticated users; externally hosted meetings and guest accounts need an explicit identity/permission path. A participant roster display name never becomes a global employee identity or a verified voiceprint.

Produce a least-privilege permission matrix for each registration: desktop sign-in, Teams app API, optional calendar discovery, meeting lifecycle/RSC, call join, raw media, transcript import, knowledge selected-resource access and background services. Document which requires admin consent, resource installation/approval, an application access policy where applicable, or delegated user authority. Do not copy a sample's complete permission set into production.

### 15.6 Automatic attendance: always for eligible meetings, not unrestricted surveillance

The required user feature is **Automatically join my eligible meetings when they actually start**. Enable it only after a clear one-time user choice and trusted organizational authorization. Once enabled and prerequisites hold, it should not demand repetitive confirmation for the same already authorized low-friction flow; organizer admission, platform rules and capture requirements still apply. Show today's eligible meetings with Skip, Pause automation and exclusions. Keep a per-occurrence override, not just a series-wide switch.

Compute eligibility from the enrolled principal, supported meeting platform/type, permitted organizer/tenant, explicit invitation/app installation or supported enrollment, resource permissions, sensitivity/exclusions, automatic-attendance policy, legal/privacy route readiness and capacity. Declined, cancelled, private/restricted or unsupported meetings do not become eligible because their URLs appear in a calendar. Do not auto-add the bot to every tenant meeting using broad discovery privileges.

Use actual meeting lifecycle events where the installed app and meeting type support them. Microsoft distinguishes actual start/end events from scheduled calendar times. Its documented event route is scoped to supported associated meetings and permissions; do not treat it as a subscription to every meeting in an organization. Calendar time can prepare metadata or schedule a bounded eligibility check, not prove the meeting is live. [R60]

Where an actual-start event is unavailable, provide the supported explicit join action or a clearly labelled, previously authorized schedule-based attempt with bounded timeout; do not advertise exact-start automation for that case. Recurring occurrences, reschedules, daylight-saving changes, instant/channel calls, late installation, event reordering and cancellation need separate tests. The later Teams capability matrix controls availability—not a hardcoded “supports Teams” boolean.

### 15.7 Join coordinator, lifecycle and duplicate suppression

Implement one server-side coordinator with a durable **metadata-only** occurrence record, authoritative lease/fencing token and idempotent event processing. Normalize tenant/platform/meeting occurrence without conflating calendar IDs, chat IDs, join URLs and call IDs. Protect any join URL as a sensitive operational secret, not public telemetry. Authenticate provider callbacks, verify validation tokens/signatures as documented, constrain origins and validate duplicate/out-of-order events.

Suggested states: DISCOVERED → ELIGIBLE → SCHEDULED/WAITING-START → JOIN-REQUESTED → LOBBY → ADMITTED → AWAITING-CAPTURE-AUTHORITY → ACTIVE → STOPPING → LEFT; with SKIPPED, CANCELLED, DENIED, UNSUPPORTED and FAILED branches. An HTTP call-created response is not proof of admission or media readiness. Read actual call state before retrying a timed-out join. [R61]

Several Métis users in one meeting must not spawn several identical assistants or duplicate transcription charges. Use a single approved assistant instance per defined tenant/occurrence scope when sharing rules permit it. Cross-tenant data separation remains mandatory; do not merge unrelated organizations' assistants merely for cost savings. Handle race, failover, host restart and lease expiration with actual call-state reconciliation, not an unexplained claim of exactly-once networking.

Respect lobby, organizer approval, blocked bot policies, waiting rooms and removal. If removed or denied, suppress automatic rejoin for that occurrence until a new explicit authorized request. Do not manipulate identity or browser automation to bypass bot protections. Use bounded retry, maximum waiting time, graceful leave on end, idle timeout after everyone leaves, and an emergency kill switch. Meeting-end authority revocation precedes slow cleanup.

A server service can attend supported enrolled meetings while the desktop is closed, but only after the always-on service and proper authorization are actually deployed. Its availability and cloud cost must be visible. It cannot awaken or control a user's computer just because their account opted into meeting notes.

### 15.8 Live Teams media: a real platform constraint

For the documented application-hosted raw-media route, Microsoft requires its .NET media library and production hosting on Windows Server in Azure. This is a separate specialized media receiver—not code that can simply be put in a Cloudflare Worker or an ordinary web tab. Qualify the current supported SDK/platform and deployment requirements before choosing the service shape. [R62]

**Proposed route:** Teams call/participant → approved Azure media receiver → authenticated Métis speech broker → Cloudflare-hosted speech → transient normalized segments → approved summary/knowledge service. Cloudflare remains the default speech engine. Azure handles the required Teams media boundary, not a second unapproved speech provider. Prefer the simplest qualified deployment meeting concurrency/recovery needs; do not mandate Kubernetes or buy infrastructure by implication.

Use current media SDK authorization and required join/media permissions for the selected scenario; application permissions, RSC and tenant policies are not interchangeable. The calls API documents multiple join routes and additional permission needs for app-hosted media; select the minimum tested combination. [R61]

**Recording and derived data are a gating issue.** Microsoft's media documentation restricts persistence of both media and derived data unless the prescribed recording-status procedure succeeds. The updateRecordingStatus documentation additionally describes a policy-based recording prerequisite. Before persisting summaries from live raw Teams media, qualify the exact supported route, solution requirements and organizational approvals. Do not claim “we keep only summaries” avoids these restrictions. [R63, R64]

If the route cannot satisfy those platform conditions, mark live automated Teams capture BLOCKED and implement permitted alternatives without mislabelling them as live participation. These may include authorized post-meeting access to a platform-generated transcript or an explicitly user-operated desktop capture with appropriate notice and permissions. Each has its own source/retention rules and user-visible limitations. Obtain current Microsoft/platform confirmation for unresolved API eligibility; a coding agent cannot manufacture eligibility by calling a similarly named endpoint.

### 15.9 One meeting source, clear notice and strict separation from device control

Select one authoritative capture source for an occurrence: qualified bot media, approved platform transcript, or explicitly enabled desktop capture. Do not transcribe the same meeting through both the bot and local system audio by default. Use a source lease and explicit handover with new generation/epoch, timestamped gap/overlap reconciliation and independent attempt accounting. A user temporarily losing network must not spawn a hidden second source.

Before processing meeting media, satisfy the platform-specific authorization/status protocol, organizational lawful-basis/notice policy and any required participant/organizer approval. Make Métis visible as a participant where the route uses a bot, and disclose what it processes, whether it persists raw media, where approved summaries go and how to stop/ask for exclusion. A name in the participant list alone is neither informed consent nor sufficient transparency.

Enforce Pause/Stop/Leave and removal throughout the lifecycle. A late participant must receive the required notice through a supported mechanism. Sensitive meeting classes and objections trigger the approved stop/exclusion procedure. Do not mute or conceal the assistant to avoid discovery. A meeting bot should be silent by default, camera/video off unless explicitly required, with no automatic chat posting beyond approved notices.

Bot-captured speech, meeting chat and a remote participant saying “Hey Métis” never authorize OS actions on any attendee's desktop. Only a trusted active desktop command session from that device can do so. Meeting-side skills operate on the meeting/knowledge scope; any handoff to a desktop requires explicit local confirmation and authenticated device binding.

### 15.10 Meeting output, sharing and other platforms

After authorized processing, save only the permitted summary/actions and provenance to the selected knowledge space. Publish a meeting-chat link or card only under an approved audience policy; enforce access when opening it, not merely at link generation. A summary of private account context must not be pasted into a mixed external meeting. Meeting attendance does not itself grant access to all future notes or all records in the organizer's knowledge space.

Post-meeting platform transcripts may already be retained by that platform/tenant. Accessing one is a distinct source and privacy profile, not evidence that Cloudflare retained a recording. Verify source availability, permission, deletion policy and the completeness of the interval retrieved. Do not automatically download full recordings to compensate for missing live access.

| Platform/route | Required Métis 2.0 disposition | Important qualification |
|---|---|---|
| Teams personal/meeting app | Implement authenticated UI and explicit meeting controls. | SSO, actual membership/audience and distinct app/media permissions. |
| Teams actual-start automatic attendance | Implement for supported, enrolled and authorized meeting types. | Correct event coverage, call state, lobby, duplicate suppression, removal and privacy readiness. |
| Teams raw application-hosted media | Implement only through the qualified native media service and permitted recording/derived-data path. | Azure/.NET and actual Microsoft/tenant approvals; absence is an explicit blocker. |
| Teams authorized post-meeting transcript | Offer as a separate source when allowed and available. | Not real-time, not proof of a visible assistant in the call, no invented transcript availability. |
| Zoom | Evaluate supported RTMS for live authorized audio; preserve provider disclosure and any required UI indication. | Zoom documents RTMS rather than a mandatory participant bot, with separate service credits. Do not label this a bot participant if it is an API integration. [R65] |
| Google Meet | Capability-gate media access on the actual supported program/account/participant requirements. | The reviewed Media API page describes Developer Preview enrollment and consent restrictions; do not advertise universal production availability. [R66] |
| Other meeting platforms | Implement a provider capability contract and a concrete scoped disposition. | Supported official APIs first. Unavailable routes are explicit; no stealth browser/scraping or universal join-by-URL promise. |

Zoom/Meet implementation may proceed behind disabled feature gates after qualification; launching them requires the same actual evidence and approvals. They are not silently treated as completed by a Teams adapter. Add platform-specific terms, permissions, region, retention, cost, notice and shutdown tests before enabling any live customer route.

### 15.11 Operations and evidence for meeting participation

Monitor eligible events received, missed/late events, join attempts, admission delay, active assistants, capture-source ownership, errors, cleanup, metadata settlement and output publication. Keep platform admission wait distinct from service-processing latency, but include it in the user-visible total. Do not label a participant waiting in a lobby as an active transcription session or productive user.
