Proposed service goals: p95 eligible actual-start event to first join request ≤5 seconds on a healthy qualified path; stop/removal blocks new processing immediately at the service boundary; no duplicate active capture source in the release concurrency suite; every completed/skipped/denied meeting has a safe explicit status. Qualify event coverage and capacity with actual tenant/platform limits, not a fixed promise for every meeting.

The release demonstration must include an enrolled scheduled Teams meeting, actual start detection, visible join/admission, required authorization/notice, real permitted audio, Cloudflare transcript, approved summary, Mantu Intelligence readback, Dust read and controlled update, and the correctly scoped Operator run/usage records. Also prove laptop-off operation, cancellation, no host, reschedule, two enrolled users, external/guest meeting, removal, revocation, service crash, duplicate event, source handover and a blocked media route. Use synthetic participants/content and authorized test tenants; never test by recording an uninformed real client meeting.

## 16. Security, no-content-retention controls and policy enforcement

### 16.1 Threat-model the actual application

Cover the renderer, preload/IPC, native helpers/XPC/named pipes, model responses, captured/observed content, browser sessions, MCP services, credential vault, device identity, provider gateways, component downloads, updates, analytics, support exports, sync and human approvals. Preserve proven controls and update old assessments when the architecture changes. A historical audit that excluded cloud SQL cannot certify the later D1 backend.

| Boundary | Required controls and tests |
|---|---|
| Renderer → privileged code | Sandboxed renderer, context isolation, narrow validated methods, trusted sender/frame checks, authorization and lifecycle checks. Never raw filesystem/shell access. |
| Audio/text → commands | Explicit trusted command session; no ordinary meeting/web text authority; stale/forged/replayed input tests. |
| Decision → action | Capability/argument validation, fresh target, exact approval, effective policy, expiry and postconditions. |
| Helper communication | Restricted channel ACL/identity, bounded schemas, no arbitrary program loading; validate native signing and process ownership assumptions. |
| Cloud requests | Authenticated scope, least-privilege secrets, rate/budget controls, allowed destination and redirect handling, no sensitive logs. |
| Downloads/updates | Trusted signed metadata, correct platform signer, digest/size checks, rollback policy, safe extraction and atomic activation. |
| Portal queries/export/cache | Server-side role/scope enforcement, correct identity binding, pagination independent from total aggregates, no cross-scope cache reuse. |
| Saved knowledge/sync | Existing encryption and authorized locations, effective retention profile, deletion/correction propagation and confidential-record exclusions. |

Electron's published security guidance is a baseline, not a replacement for testing Métis's exposed APIs. Keep web content untrusted even when rendered in a sandbox. Never rely on an LLM safety classification as the only defense against excessive agency, prompt injection or inappropriate output execution. [R20, R21]

### 16.2 Secrets and installation identity

Enterprise artifacts must exclude reusable shared provider/account keys. Audit actual packaged resources and build-time injection paths without printing recovered credentials. If an embedded live shared key is found, report privately and rotate through the authorized operational process; do not commit it as “proof.” Maintain per-device/principal authentication, revocation, server-side provider vaulting and tested rate limits. User-supplied personal keys or approved CLI subscriptions are separate explicitly authorized routes, never quiet fallbacks for a revoked corporate seat.

Keep signing secrets in approved CI secret/HSM/signing-service facilities. Do not ask the user to paste keys or certificates into a chat document. Rotate credentials and update validation roots under reviewed procedures; a reconnect failure is not permission to hardcode a bypass.

### 16.3 Egress and captured content

Maintain a route/transport matrix covering Node fetch, Chromium, WebSocket, raw HTTPS SDK clients, native network APIs, child processes, CLI providers, model/asset downloads and MCP. The earlier egress wrapper did not cover all of these. Enforce application policy where technically possible and name endpoint/network-management controls where needed. Do not claim global egress enforcement from one fetch wrapper.

Screenshots, DOM text, accessible labels, retrieved documents, tool results, instructions embedded in files and meeting speech are untrusted observations. Minimize them, remove unnecessary secrets and scope collection to the active task. Do not capture the whole desktop continuously just to improve routing. Treat data residency/vendor retention separately from local summary-only saving, including gateway request logging and provider recording defaults.

### 16.4 Consent, permissions and privacy expectations

Request OS permissions at a relevant user gesture with a clear explanation. Keep capture status visible and truthful. Denial must produce a usable typed/manual path, not a fake success or permission bypass. Protect camera output and browser credentials separately from microphone access. High-impact external actions require exact user approval and may be prohibited by organization policy even when the user requests them.

Preserve summary/action-only storage for managed profiles, including temporary files, queues, exports, model traces and external graph helpers. Do not store raw text in analytics under a harmless-looking “preview” field. Clear relevant caches/buffers on sign-out/revocation/deletion while preserving existing data according to the authorized retention/migration plan.

### 16.5 Observability without surveillance

Capture metadata necessary to diagnose failures: safe event category, correlation IDs, version, elapsed time, backend/route, outcome, completeness and policy reason. Define sampling and cardinality limits. Separate diagnostic sampling from accounting: a sampled trace cannot be the only usage ledger.

Provide tested alerts for critical crash/launch failure, integrity failure, authorization anomalies, accounting lag, provider outage, exhausted budget and component-download failure. Verify incident ownership, a kill switch for unsafe capabilities, canary rollback and recovery procedures. Local log hash chains are tamper-evidence within their threat model, not absolute proof against a compromised OS account.


### 16.6 Cloudflare no-content-retention contract

**Define the claim precisely.** Audio, interim/final transcripts, command text, vocabulary, prompts, generated responses and content-derived embeddings/recognizable hashes are confidential content. During authorized processing they may exist transiently in the required memory/transport buffers; they must not be written to Cloudflare-controlled application storage, response caches, payload logs, durable work queues or diagnostic exports. Approved numerical/categorical usage metadata and verified user/device administration records are a separate class with finite access/retention rules. Retaining that ledger means the product must not advertise “Cloudflare stores no data whatsoever.”

Cloudflare's Workers AI documentation says it does not use Customer Content for training or service improvement without explicit consent, and identifies storage services as a way content can be persisted. AI Gateway documentation separately describes persistent request/response logging that is enabled by default. These are different controls; a no-training statement does not establish a complete no-retention deployment. [R42, R43]

#### 16.6.1 Route qualification before real user content

Create an evidence record for each enabled speech/inference route: actual model ID, hosting operator, transport/endpoint, account and region restrictions, applicable service/model terms, no-training and content-retention basis, configured logs/caches, approved metadata, review date and owner. Qualify Cloudflare-hosted Workers AI separately from provider-native BYOK or Unified Billing. Where contractual meaning or partner handling is unclear, obtain authorized supplier/privacy confirmation; do not accept terms, declare legal compliance or infer undocumented deletion behavior on behalf of the organization.

Deepgram's direct-service documentation describes an opt-out that changes its content retention, while request metadata remains. Do not assume that direct-Deepgram flags or terms establish identical Cloudflare-hosted handling, and do not pass `mip_opt_out=true` through an endpoint unless its support and meaning are verified. This reference is a route-distinction warning, not an instruction to add direct Deepgram as a fallback. [R48]

Cloudflare's generic Unified Billing `zdr` setting is documented for particular upstream configurations and explicitly does not control AI Gateway logs. It must not be treated as universal proof for Nova-3 or arbitrary BYOK. An unsupported-ZDR fallback is prohibited for this product; block the route rather than quietly accept weaker treatment. [R47]

Represent privacy readiness as `UNREVIEWED`, `CONFIGURED`, `VERIFIED` or `BLOCKED`, with evidence tied to the actual deployment/config revision. Synthetic non-sensitive probes may run to verify controls. Real customer-content sessions cannot start before the route is approved and the deployed controls pass their readiness gate. A boolean saved in a UI is not the evidence.

#### 16.6.2 Persistence denylist and permitted stores

| Surface | Mandatory content policy | Allowed alternative |
|---|---|---|
| AI Gateway payload/log collection | Disable complete per-request log collection on sensitive routes by default. No stored prompts, audio, transcript or response payload. | Dedicated Operator metadata ledger. Metadata-only gateway logs require separate approval and end-to-end transport verification. |
| AI response caches / Worker Cache API / CDN | No content caching, including related transcript-derived requests or error responses. | Public immutable installers/models may be cached under a distinct asset route. |
| Worker logs, live-tail, Logpush, traces, APM | No request/response/frame dumps, transcripts, vocabulary or tokens/credentials. Error paths must be projected before logging. | Safe event codes, versions, opaque IDs, counters and timings. |
| R2, KV, D1, Durable Object storage, Queues | No recordings, transcripts, prompts or inference results; no encrypted content blob disguised as telemetry. | Approved component assets, policy/session metadata and minimized accounting data. |
| Exceptions/crash reports and support bundles | Exclude audio buffers, payloads, URLs with secrets, transcript previews and heap/core dumps that can contain content. | Sanitized diagnostics and reproduction using synthetic fixtures. |
| Retry/reconnect handling | Volatile, bounded current-session buffers only; no deferred audio jobs, callbacks storing results, recording archive or spool. | Metadata-only repair of usage settlement. |
| User-approved saved knowledge | Keep authorized summaries/actions and scoped wiki/graph projections in the selected approved knowledge environment; no new Cloudflare content copy. | Section 17 governs sharing/migration, readable projection, correction and retention. |
| Published skill software/configuration | Only reviewed, correctly classified definitions/schemas/control artifacts may persist in the authorized control plane. | Confidential attached knowledge and populated run prompts/results are not software-artifact exceptions; section 12.7 applies. |

A Durable Object may coordinate session identity, concurrency or budgets if needed; its persistent storage must remain metadata-only. Its existence does not justify persisting audio for reliable delivery. R2 component distribution uses a different trust/cache policy than speech; never globally disable public asset caching while trying to protect inference content, or enable content caching through a shared wildcard rule.

#### 16.6.3 Exact controls to implement and verify

Provision or reuse a deliberately configured sensitive-traffic gateway; do not rely on auto-created `default` behavior for private speech. Preserve unrelated gateways/settings, and export sanitized before/after configuration. Configure gateway logging off and response caching off **before** a test that sends protected content. On a route that traverses AI Gateway, apply supported per-request controls in trusted server code as defense in depth:

```text
cf-aig-collect-log: false
cf-aig-collect-log-payload: false
cf-aig-skip-cache: true
```

For the documented Worker binding, use its actual supported gateway options such as `collectLog: false` and `skipCache: true`, with a reviewed named gateway. Do not invent a `collectLogPayload` binding property merely because an HTTP header exists. [R43–R46]

These headers/options are not a universal end-to-end privacy switch. Verify their actual effect on the selected **real-time WebSocket handshake and frame handling**, not only a text HTTP request. Strip/override client attempts to enable logs/cache or redirect to a different gateway/model. Unsupported or ineffective controls block that candidate route. Never just add the header to a response; it must govern the upstream request where supported.

Mark HTTP control/inference responses non-cacheable using appropriate `Cache-Control` settings and route rules; exclude bodies/frames from all logging and tracing layers. `Cache-Control: no-store` alone does not disable server-side logging. A one-second TTL or later deletion is not equivalent to never persisting content. No vendor/model training consent or feedback upload may be auto-enabled to earn a discount.

Disable session replay and content-bearing debug tooling for production capture, including Electron/native crash-report integrations and backend log-tail consumers. Use an allowlist projection before every event sink. A redaction regex alone is insufficient because a missed word can still be confidential. Do not store hashes or embeddings of short utterances as proof of identity or verification; use opaque generated event IDs.

#### 16.6.4 Prove what is controllable; document the remaining supplier boundary

Run content-sentinel tests with synthetic phrases/audio across successful live/batch routes, errors, retries, cancellation, disconnects, rejected auth, revocation, optional second-pass processing and Jev/generative follow-on requests. Inspect all configured controlled sinks (gateway logs/cache, Worker logs/export destinations, D1, KV/R2/DO/queues, traces and support bundles) and prove only allowed metadata appears. Cover both HTTP and WebSocket paths, not a single happy path. Never copy customer content into audit evidence in order to prove its absence elsewhere.

Combine this with explicit configuration readback, source-level negative tests on log/storage paths, egress tests and approved provider documentation/contractual evidence. Absence from an accessible log view alone cannot prove anything about inaccessible provider infrastructure, OS swap or hardware memory. Do not claim secure erasure of managed-language memory or universal physical zeroization. Bound lifetimes, release references, avoid application-controlled durable copies and document platform limitations.

Drift detection must compare the live privacy-relevant configuration with its approved revision without using real speech payloads. Run at deployment and on an authorized periodic job with recorded cadence; this document itself creates no background job. Unexpected enabled logging, cache, model destination, persistence sink or privilege widens the trust boundary: fail new content sessions closed, alert the operator with metadata, terminate affected sessions safely as policy requires, and require requalification. Typed local/manual functions remain usable.

If pre-existing content logs are found, stop further collection, restrict access and prepare an exact, authorized deletion/retention-remediation plan. Do not erase unrelated records, override legal holds or claim that deletion removes earlier training or every backup. Historical remediation and future no-content-retention qualification are separate evidence items.

#### 16.6.5 User-facing privacy language

Once verified, use accurate language such as: “Speech is processed through the approved Cloudflare route. Métis is configured not to persist audio or transcript content in Cloudflare logs, caches or application storage. Authorized usage metadata is retained for administration. Saved summaries follow your selected storage policy.” Tailor this to the actual reviewed route and supplier assurance; do not display it when readiness is blocked or supplier handling is unresolved.

The requested product goal is **no content retention by the speech processing chain**, not a marketing badge inferred from a header. Codex implements and tests the application controls, produces the supplier/configuration evidence checklist, and reports any unresolved assurance as a release blocker for that privacy claim. Only authorized business/privacy owners can resolve contractual gaps; code cannot fabricate that approval.

### 16.7 GDPR and enterprise governance: implement controls, do not invent certification

Teams, Entra ID, Cloudflare, OneDrive and Dust are components in a processing chain. None makes Métis GDPR-compliant merely by being present. The controller must establish the purposes, lawful basis, parties, safeguards and ability to demonstrate compliance. Use this section as a deployment evidence checklist, reviewed by the authorized privacy/legal owner, not as legal advice or a certification. [R67, R68, R71]

Document separate purposes for meeting attendance, transcription, summaries, shared knowledge, contextual skills, service security and usage accounting. Define controller/processor roles and approved sub-processors across Microsoft, Cloudflare, Dust, hosting and model providers. Verify actual contractual terms, locations, transfers, training/retention conditions and scope of customer instructions. Entra authentication or an organizer's invitation is not a universal lawful basis for recording every attendee.

Determine whether a DPIA and employee-representative/works-council consultation are required for the deployment, with a reasoned owner decision. Do not assume employee consent is freely given just because a checkbox exists [R69]; where consent is the chosen basis, address withdrawal and power imbalance, and where another basis is used, document it transparently. Do not invent a universal country-independent meeting-recording rule.

Provide clear notice before participation/capture, purpose and recipient information, retention and sharing destinations, a way to stop/exclude, and contact/rights information. Keep consent or policy acknowledgments versioned and purpose-scoped; never bury sensitive processing inside a model-install toggle. Automatically joining an eligible meeting does not authorize a different downstream purpose such as employee scoring or marketing reuse.

Data rights cover approved summaries, action items, relationship graphs, inferred claims, derived indexes, published wiki pages and configured external replicas—not only recordings. Implement authorized access/export, correction, restriction and deletion workflows with identity verification, deadlines from the applicable policy, source lineage and honest completion status. Deletion from an app index is not deletion from M365, Dust or backups unless confirmed by the relevant connector/procedure. Legal holds and independent controller obligations remain explicit.

Prohibit employee productivity scoring, emotion recognition, inferred protected traits, biometric speaker identity and covert surveillance as default Métis capabilities. Usage dashboards support operations and budgeting; they must not claim to measure employees' contribution from tokens, speaking time or online status. A later request for materially different processing requires a separate product/privacy review.

Keep corporate skill **software definitions** distinct from run payloads. Explicitly classified templates may persist as control artifacts; private prompts, examples populated with client data and model responses may not be stored in Cloudflare by being renamed templates. Approved saved summaries/graph content belong only in the selected authorized knowledge environment. Make this storage matrix visible in IT documentation and the user's effective Privacy page.

Require release evidence for role-based access, tenant/source/audience isolation, minimization, finite retention, consent/notice behavior, revocation, subject-rights propagation, third-party configuration and incident response. A human privacy owner signs off the deployment decision; Codex implements and tests the controls and records unresolved blockers. Never display a GDPR-compliant badge simply because tests passed or Microsoft SSO worked.

## 17. Mantu Intelligence: governed knowledge, wiki and Dust read/write

### 17.1 The authority contract

Treat “LLM Wi-Fi” as the requested shared, wiki-like knowledge access layer: a place where authorized people and agents can find current meeting context without repeatedly reconstructing it. This is a product metaphor, not a network protocol or a claim that an LLM is an oracle. **Mantu Intelligence is the governed system of record and access surface; a model or generated graph is never absolute truth.**

The authority hierarchy is: authorized source record and explicit owner decisions → versioned canonical facts/actions → generated wiki and graph projections → retrieved context → generated response. A correction, source removal or permission change propagates downward. A polished answer cannot silently rewrite the upper layers. Where a source is missing, uncertain, contradicted or no longer retained, expose that limitation.

Retain the selected second brain and its existing data/OneDrive compatibility. Do not create a second canonical knowledge database just because a graph engine is available. Define a single logical record owner, even if a controlled service and user devices cache projections. An external CRM may remain authoritative for its commercial fields; a meeting can propose an update but cannot overrule that source through a summary.

### 17.2 Build on the wiki exporter that already exists

The inspected `src/main/brain/publish.ts` already exports Dust-readable meeting/entity markdown and an index under the selected meetings folder's `wiki/` directory. It is gated by `publishBrainPages` and intentionally writes plaintext, irrespective of transcript encryption. It also preserves stricter human-verification gates for amounts and close dates. These are source facts, not proof of the deployed feature's behavior. [R52]

Preserve the useful exporter, stable source identifiers, exclusions and field rules. Before enabling sharing, inventory its real outputs, who can read the folder, whether sync/indexing copies exist and what deletion does. Never imply that a readable wiki inherits ATKENC encryption simply because the source notes were encrypted. A user must see the exact published audience and an explicit sharing choice governed by organizational policy.

Generated `AGENTS.md`, `CLAUDE.md` and other wiki files are retrieved content—not authority to override the calling agent's instructions, tool permissions or system policy. Delimit their content and treat embedded instructions as untrusted. Do not train the assistant to obey an exported page merely because its filename resembles a coding-agent instruction file.

Dust must not edit those generated pages as its canonical write path: regeneration would discard edits or bring back superseded information. It writes validated canonical mutations or proposals through the knowledge service, which then regenerates projections. A generated-page edit discovered from sync becomes a clearly attributed proposed edit/conflict, never an automatic policy or verified-fact change.

### 17.3 Storage and always-available access without violating Cloudflare privacy

Use three separate data classes:

| Class | Durable home and access | Never confuse it with |
|---|---|---|
| Authorized knowledge content | Existing selected second brain; an explicitly approved M365/OneDrive/SharePoint-backed shared configuration where needed. | Cloudflare's metadata ledger or unrestricted recordings. |
| Derived wiki/graph/search projections | Rebuildable, access-scoped material in the approved knowledge environment, with explicit retention. | Independent evidence or an automatically authoritative database. |
| Operational metadata | Operator's approved IDs, versions, counters, latency, policy and audit records. | Meeting titles, summaries, transcripts, embeddings or free-form model responses. |

**Recommended shared-service deployment:** an Entra-protected knowledge API in an approved organizational hosting environment, accessing the already selected M365 knowledge location with scoped permissions. Use a team-owned SharePoint library only when approved as the shared location; do not move a personal OneDrive silently. Microsoft documents resource-selected permissions that require both consent and explicit resource grants; qualify the exact API/permission combination used, without defaulting to tenant-wide read/write. [R57]

This service makes authorized knowledge available to Dust and Teams while an employee's laptop is asleep. It stores canonical updates/projections in the approved knowledge environment, not raw speech or inference payloads in Cloudflare. Operator may authenticate/control transient calls, but its logs, caches, D1, R2, KV, queues and persistent coordination storage stay free of knowledge content under section 16.6.

A purely local or device-key-encrypted vault is not automatically cloud-readable. Choose and document one of two honest states: (a) a qualified always-on service with an explicitly authorized migration/sharing and key-management design; or (b) an authenticated device bridge that works only while that device is reachable. Do not upload device master keys, export the entire vault in plaintext or pretend a sleeping laptop is a reliable server. Always-on enterprise sharing requires the first state for its advertised availability; missing authorization is a concrete blocker, not a reason to silently weaken encryption.

Use the least new infrastructure needed: existing M365 records and a small service may suffice. A managed database is justified only by tested concurrency/query requirements and an approved data-location decision. Do not impose Neo4j, a vector database, PostgreSQL or a large Graphify runtime on every desktop merely to draw a graph. Metadata-only job queues can carry opaque record/revision references; workers fetch authorized content just in time.

### 17.4 Canonical record model and evidence provenance

Extend existing schemas where possible. Each record needs a stable tenant/space-qualified ID, record type, version, source owner, actor type, source-system ID/revision, applicable audience/ACL, sensitivity, event/observed/effective dates, freshness state, retention policy, and references to permitted evidence. Fields with materially different provenance or permissions require field-level handling or separate records.

Separate meeting occurrence, authorized summary, decision, action/commitment, account, person, skill output and proposed correction. A recurring meeting series is not the same object as one occurrence. A participant display name is not a verified Entra principal. A model-generated task is not proof that the proposed owner accepted it.

Represent knowledge states explicitly: SOURCE-OBSERVED, EXTRACTED, INFERRED, HUMAN-VERIFIED, PINNED, EDITED, DISPUTED, SUPERSEDED, STALE and DELETED. Map these to existing enums rather than creating conflicting parallel state machines. Model probability is separate from evidence state. Human verification has actor, time and exact field/revision scope; it is not an unlimited certification of an entire record.

Keep amounts, dates, ownership, contract commitments and other consequential fields behind appropriate provenance/approval. A quoted “maybe 50,000” cannot become a verified budget. Preserve uncertainty and alternative interpretations. Different accounts with similar names must not merge because a model believes they are probably identical. Merge requires constrained matching, an authorized preview and reversible provenance.

Prevent evidence laundering: generated text cannot cite an earlier generated page as independent confirmation of the same claim. Track originating sources through summaries/graphs and detect cycles. A deleted recording cannot remain represented as available primary evidence; a retained approved summary may be cited as that summary, not as a verbatim transcript.

### 17.5 Graphify as a compiler and exploration layer

Qualify the requested `mysticalsin/graphify` fork at an exact commit and configuration. Its inspected documentation describes graph/report outputs and distinguishes extracted from inferred edges; that is useful for explainable navigation but does not provide the entire Métis authority, permission or mutation system. [R53]

Use Graphify only behind a narrow projection adapter: canonical permitted records in → bounded derived nodes/edges out → validate provenance/audience → atomically publish a versioned projection. Prefer existing structured `.brain` relationships when they already represent the required facts. Model-assisted inference is optional, cost-limited and never required to reconstruct already explicit links.

An edge carries source record/revision, origin (explicit extraction or inference), review status, permitted audience, timestamp and invalidation dependency. A derived edge may not widen access beyond its sources. Partition computation and export by authorized space; filtering only after a full graph has reached the client/model is too late. Node names, counts, paths and missing-node explanations can reveal restricted relationships, so protect them too.

Require incremental rebuilds, resumable reference-only jobs, idempotent source processing, deletion/tombstone handling and safe full reconstruction. Publish graph/index revisions atomically so clients cannot read half of an update. On failure, label the previous projection stale; do not return it as current truth. Quarantine malformed/oversized documents, path traversal and instruction-bearing pages. Keep the developer code graph separate from customer meeting knowledge.

### 17.6 Retrieval and the context builder

Authorize the principal, task, space and output audience before search. Resolve the relevant meeting/account/person with permitted metadata. Retrieve a small set of source-backed candidates, add only relevant graph neighbors, assemble a bounded evidence packet and check access again before returning or generating. Start with existing search/entity retrieval; add semantic/hybrid retrieval only after a measured gap, not by default.

Each evidence packet states source/revision, date, provenance, excerpt or structured fields where permitted, coverage/limitations and a citation handle. Keep a hard context/token budget, bounded graph depth and deterministic ranking inputs. Do not load every meeting into every prompt. If insufficient, clarify or explain the gap instead of filling it from model memory.

Cache keys include authenticated scope, space, requested audience, policy version, source/projection revision, time interval and relevant skill/template/model version. Permission revocation invalidates eligibility immediately at the read boundary; cache expiration alone is not the revocation control. After a mutation, support read-your-write through the canonical record even while projections catch up. Display “update committed; search rebuilding” rather than a false unchanged record.

For Teams responses, default to private delivery to the authenticated requester. Sharing a briefing to a meeting chat/stage requires all included sources to permit that audience and the appropriate explicit publish action. Membership in a meeting is not permission to read the requester's full account history.

### 17.7 Dust connection, identity and read capabilities

Implement a remote MCP interface over the same governed knowledge service, not a second Dust-only datastore. Dust documents custom MCP connections and both personal and shared credentials. Prefer a qualified personal OAuth flow for user-specific data; shared credentials mean shared authority and must be restricted accordingly. [R54, R55]

Use a server-validated identity and audience-bound access token. Derive the effective tenant/user/agent/service principal from that identity, never from `userEmail`, `tenantId` or `agentName` supplied in a tool argument. For application/service calls with no delegated person, enforce explicitly granted service scopes and record that actor honestly. A workspace-wide token must not masquerade as each user.

Define small tool contracts (names below are desired logical APIs, not claims of existing endpoints):

| Tool | Capability | Authorization boundary |
|---|---|---|
| `knowledge.search` | Scoped search of meetings, accounts, commitments and approved pages. | Server limits space, audience, filters, page size and fields. |
| `knowledge.get` | Retrieve a canonical record and permitted evidence at a stated revision. | Revalidate access on every read; no guessed file-path access. |
| `knowledge.changes` | Incremental change/tombstone feed from an opaque scoped cursor. | Cursor binds principal/scope; no other-space metadata leaks. |
| `knowledge.explain` | Return source/provenance behind a claim or graph edge. | Only evidence the requester may view; no hidden chain-of-thought. |
| `knowledge.propose_update` | Create a typed attributed change proposal. | Cannot set approval, ACL, financial verification or arbitrary source identity. |
| `actions.create` / `actions.update_owned` | Make permitted low-risk action changes. | Exact role/field scope, ownership and version checks; consequential fields may require review. |
| `knowledge.commit_change` | Commit an approved permitted mutation. | Service checks current approval/record revision; an agent cannot approve itself. |

Bound tool definitions and responses; avoid full-corpus downloads. Return authentic source handles that the UI can resolve under authorization. Read-only tools have no side effects other than approved metadata logging. A tool named search must not secretly backfill an external index, change policy or start a new recording.

Dust may retain conversation/tool results according to its actual workspace and service configuration. Review the selected workspace region, processing/retention terms, permissions and deletion features before sending sensitive knowledge. Cloudflare log suppression does not disable Dust history. Prohibit automatic bulk mirroring into a broadly accessible Dust space. A selected connector mirror needs its own scoped retention/revocation qualification.

### 17.8 Dust writes must actually work—and must survive synchronization

Provide real allowed writes, not a permanent read-only integration dressed as collaboration. Low-risk user-authorized updates can commit immediately under policy; high-impact corrections, pinned facts, shared ownership changes or publication generate a reviewable proposal. Make the difference visible to the agent and user.

A mutation contains record ID, expected revision, typed patch, idempotency key, evidence references and reason. Authenticate actor/space separately. Use compare-and-swap/ETag preconditions in the actual backing store; where Microsoft Graph supports the chosen operation, a stale `If-Match` produces a conflict rather than an overwrite. Qualify the specific content-write API instead of assuming every file API has identical semantics. [R58]

Use states such as RECEIVED, VALIDATED, PENDING-APPROVAL, COMMITTED, PROJECTION-PENDING, VISIBLE, CONFLICT or REJECTED. HTTP acceptance or a proposal ID is not a completed update. After commit, read back the exact changed fields/revision; then schedule derived projection refresh. Do not treat search-index lag as write failure and repeat the side effect.

Retry only safe idempotent operations. Distinguish an already committed write from an ambiguous timeout. Concurrent Dust agents and a human editor must not overwrite one another silently. Preserve unrelated fields, verify exact Unicode/numeric values and reject arbitrary JSON paths or injected instructions. Record minimal mutation metadata and the permitted source revision, not a duplicate plaintext payload in Operator logs.

Edits to authoritative external systems are separate capabilities. Creating an internal action does not automatically create a Teams task, send mail or update a CRM. Those need their own target, permissions, approval, idempotency and postcondition tests.

### 17.9 Freshness, deletion, confidentiality and repair

Support additions, revisions, moves, merges, deletions and access changes from both clients and services. Track source IDs rather than unstable filenames. Reconcile event streams with scoped delta/change enumeration so a missed event does not permanently lose an update. A periodic reconciliation cadence is an implemented service job with owner and evidence, not a promise of background activity in this chat.

Deletion/revocation propagates to graph/wiki pages, search/embedding indexes, caches, saved context packets, shared links and configured Dust replicas where applicable. Block reads immediately using source eligibility/tombstones even while asynchronous physical cleanup is pending. Prevent a stale device or restored backup from re-publishing a deleted record. Legal holds/backup retention require the approved policy and honest limits; do not destroy unrelated data or promise erasure from third-party systems that have not confirmed it.

Confidential meetings are excluded by default from team publication and generated knowledge prompts unless explicitly authorized. A mixed-source aggregate cannot expose restricted numbers through counts, graph degree or a trend. Treat derived scores and relationship maps as potentially personal/confidential data, not anonymized simply because raw speech is absent.

Repair tools show source/projection drift, conflicting edits, missing permissions, dead citations and failed synchronization. They operate only on app-owned records, with preview, scoped authority, idempotency and rollback where possible. Avoid a “rebuild everything” button that deletes evidence or reindexes all private files.

### 17.10 Intelligence experience and concrete quality gates

Default to a clear briefing, not a force-directed graph. Provide Today, Meetings, Actions, People & accounts, and a review inbox; the graph is an exploration view. A question yields What changed, What is confirmed, What is uncertain and What needs action, with source dates and an evidence drawer. Let users correct a field or resolve a conflict in place without technical graph terminology.

Proposed qualification goals, to validate against the corpus and service envelope: p95 authorized metadata/search response ≤1 second excluding generation; explicit read-your-write after commit; ordinary projection refresh p95 ≤60 seconds with a visible watermark; all stale/conflict states surfaced; zero unauthorized disclosures in the release negative suite. These are engineering targets, not population guarantees or vendor SLAs. A faster answer that hides missing permissions is a failure.

Build a permissioned gold set of real-shaped meeting summaries and structured facts covering multiple accounts, similar names, corrections, contradictory commitments, lost sources, negation, changing audiences and multilingual queries. Measure citation correctness, factual support, appropriate abstention, retrieval coverage, freshness, mutation success and access isolation—not just answer fluency. Include at least one end-to-end sequence: approved meeting summary → Mantu Intelligence → Dust read → authorized Dust write → human UI readback → correction → Dust sees correction → access revoked → Dust/graph no longer expose it.

### 17.11 Make Jev and Laya useful in business Intelligence, not only desktop control

Preserve the original three-layer briefing contract. **Facts** are calculated by code from authorized source revisions: counts, amounts, dates, owners, overdue/unassigned commitments and changes. **Assessments** use the configured Jev/Laya decision backend with a versioned rubric over that bounded evidence. **Explanations** use deterministic templates or an existing approved generative route and cite supporting records. An assessment cannot overwrite a CRM field or invent its own evidence. [R72]

Required questions include overall status, changes since the last review, accounts/projects needing attention, blockers, overdue or unassigned commitments, today's priorities and evidence behind each conclusion. Support only authorized personal/team/account/project/country/business-unit/organizational scopes; record coverage when the user cannot view the whole proposed aggregate. Never retrieve broader underlying records and rely on later redaction.

Represent business status separately from freshness and coverage: on-track on stale evidence is not the same thing as freshly confirmed on-track. Include needs-attention, blocked, conflicting-evidence and insufficient-information outcomes. Rubric updates have pinned versions and held-out evaluation. Test actual Jev and Laya classifications in Intelligence, not just an “enabled” badge or a command demo.

Use deterministic decimal/currency/timezone rules. Pipeline is not revenue, unconverted currencies cannot be summed, and missing values are not zero. Refresh changed records incrementally and use **clock-driven invalidation** for deadlines and stale windows: a commitment can become overdue at midnight without an edit. Preserve event time, source age and analysis time separately. An old asynchronous assessment cannot replace a newer revision. [R72]

### 17.12 Canonical commit, synchronization and projection repair

Do not assume a local file rename, an M365 write, a metadata event and a graph publish form one transaction. Select the actual canonical write boundary and enforce its supported precondition. If the backing operation supports atomic data-plus-outbox commit, use it; otherwise make canonical revision enumeration/delta reconciliation the repair source after an event is lost. Never add an unauthorized content database merely to make a queue transactional.

A successful local save reports local success only. Distinguish sync pending, remote verified, conflict and failed. Remote verification binds the correct organization/drive/item and returned revision, not a directory whose name contains OneDrive. Dust write success is based on canonical readback; the search watermark may remain behind without retrying the write. Preserve exact approved Unicode content and unrelated fields. [R72, R73]

Rebuilding projections cannot resurrect a deleted record or widen its audience. After a backup restore, apply revocation/tombstone reconciliation before serving restored content. A stale device reconnect must not overwrite newer authoritative state. Migration and deletion tests cover permission changes between retrieval, generation, commit and publication, not just before the first API call.

## 18. Refactoring and persistent engineering context

### 18.1 Refactor after each affected component has a regression baseline

Apply the supplied refactoring skill once retrieved and reviewed. Use Ponytail as complementary reuse-first guidance, not a license to golf code. Fewer lines, fewer dependencies and faster navigation are useful only when behavior, validation, error handling, security, accessibility and diagnosability remain correct. [R37]

Inventory oversized modules, duplicate state machines, repeated validation, accidental cross-layer imports, dynamic/string-based dependencies, duplicated vendor SDKs, heavy render bundles and unnecessary always-on loops. Refactor by responsibility with small patches and characterization tests. Avoid a new abstraction for every two-line similarity; prefer existing platform/library capabilities where they genuinely meet requirements.

Keep urgent fixes separate from broad file moves so reviewers can reason about behavioral changes. A file move does not demonstrate a cleaner architecture. Use dependency-boundary tests and shared-schema fixtures to prevent reconnection of unsafe paths after refactoring.

### 18.2 One useful source graph, not three competing memories

Evaluate `mysticalsin/code-review-graph` first for structural indexing and change impact. Add Graphify's document/ADR relationships only if they improve an identified gap. Read the requested forks and provenance, pin the reviewed implementation and keep dependencies local to the engineering environment. Do not ship Python graph tools in the Métis user installer. [R35, R36]

Index identity must include repository/worktree, full commit, dirty-file hashes, parser/schema/config version and exclusion rules. Refresh after edits, branch switches, renames and deletes. Reject or mark stale results. Model dynamic relationships—IPC channel, preload method, Worker route, D1 table, generated bundle, feature flag and packaging resource—as explicit verified supplemental edges where static parsing cannot establish them.

A graph query returns bounded source-linked context: defining symbol, caller/callee/consumer candidates, relevant tests, confidence/edge origin and source revision. It does not prove a path is absent or a test actually executed. Read the real code before editing. For a known simple file, direct scoped search may be cheaper than graph traversal.

### 18.3 Durable project handoff

Reuse existing project locations or maintain a small `docs/metis-2/` index with task status, owners, decisions, test evidence and continuation notes. Keep a compact `CURRENT.md` containing branch/SHA, dirty-work ownership, active task, relevant requirements, exact commands/results, blockers and next operation. Record lessons as specific regressions and preventative tests, not a growing essay of generic advice.

Preserve immutable security/release/data rules in the always-loaded instructions. Optional rule selection, semantic compaction and supervisory agents may help locate context but cannot discard approvals, unresolved failures, target identities, current branch constraints, requirement IDs or known limitations. Never send unrestricted shell history, secrets or the entire repository conversation to Jev merely to reduce later tokens.

### 18.4 Measure real developer savings

Compare full-task performance with and without the graph on the same tasks, competent scoped-search baseline and comparable coding environment. Count indexing, incremental refresh, graph responses, context reads, inference calls, wrong turns, elapsed time and correctness. Include cold and resumed sessions. Report unavailable token metrics honestly; file-token estimates are not API bills. Do not repeat another repository's maximum saving as a Métis guarantee.

### 18.5 Complete repository audit and release-lane ownership

Inventory all shipped workspaces and build variants: desktop main/preload/renderers, native-app/helpers, Operator frontend/Worker/D1, Intelligence, license server, cloud proxy, resource provisioning, CI/release/update workflows and contributed tools. Record parsed, inspected, tested and unassessed coverage separately; a root dependency audit does not automatically cover every workspace or native asset. Review secrets, vulnerable dependencies, licenses, asset supply chain and applicable static/security tests at the actual lockfiles and build graph. Existing approved security tools may be reused; do not install every reference project or overwrite hooks as part of an audit.

Inspect approved newer GitHub UI implementations and actual branch diffs, rather than assume main is always the newest user-facing design. Carry narrow fixes and necessary dependencies into the designated release lane with explicit provenance. Preserve historical evidence, but rerun all affected checks after integration. A source-only code graph never certifies runtime wiring, final credentials, installed UI or a signed artifact.

Use surgical characterization-backed refactoring as work proceeds. TASK-061 is the integration-wide architecture/complexity review and final regression sweep, not a reason to defer every cleanup until a meeting-provider legal approval arrives. Apply the exact supplied skill once identified and authorized; record any remaining skill-input gap rather than claiming compliance with a guessed skill.

## 19. Reference-repository adoption decisions

All listed references must receive a recorded disposition: ADAPT PATTERN, QUALIFY COMPONENT, ENGINEERING ONLY or DO NOT ADOPT. The following are the proposed starting decisions based on README/source reconnaissance, not full security audits. Check the actual license, dependency tree, hooks, network destinations, maintenance, tests and selected commit before executing or copying. User-provided approximate star counts are not acceptance evidence and are intentionally not repeated.

| Reference | Proposed use for Métis 2.0 | Boundary / rejection criterion |
|---|---|---|
| `browser-use/jev-ultrafast` [R23] | ADAPT PATTERN: dynamic indexed action/target space, batched decisions, fresh DOM references, verified outcomes. | Do not adopt unguarded debugging/profile access or claim its one-task demo timing as general reliability. |
| `awlevin/typesafe-computer-use` [R24] | ADAPT PATTERN: native accessibility observations, bounded actions and explicit capture/control permissions. | Mac-specific Python demo is not a Windows adapter. Avoid broad screenshot collection or treating confidence as approval. |
| `droidrun/mobile-jev` [R25] | ENGINEERING REFERENCE: state-based outcome verification and exclusive device ownership. | Mobilerun/device charges and Android hosting are separate. No mobile control dependency or automatic product-scope expansion. |
| `moritzkremb/jev-voice-browser` [R26] | ADAPT PATTERN: partial-intent preparation, candidate text spans and a compact feedback loop. | Do not execute risky incomplete speech or import its separate Web Speech microphone/Chromium stack into the core. |
| `tamaratran/fast-jev-compaction` [R27] | ENGINEERING ONLY, opt-in trial: retain exact useful tool call/result pairs. | Its process can delete/truncate history and send broad context to Jev. Protect invariant constraints, provenance and data policy; prove net savings. |
| `thruwire/foreman` [R28] | ENGINEERING ONLY: advisory progress/test-gap supervision. | An experimental classifier cannot approve a merge, certify tests, clear a release gate or replace an independent reviewer. |
| `devagrawal09/jev-review` [R29] | ENGINEERING ONLY: evidence-focused change review and layered review implementation ideas. | Validate every finding against source/tests; no automatic production authority or broad unapproved code upload. |
| `gargpratyush/jev-router` [R30] | ENGINEERING ONLY: optional per-turn coding-model selection. | This is a CLI routing project, not the Métis decision gateway. Preserve native CLI auth, permissions, sessions and user choices. |
| `prismhq/jev-router` [R31] | ADAPT PATTERN: eligibility/capability filtering before model choice. | Distinct from the CLI router. Do not install a duplicate LiteLLM gateway unless an ADR proves the existing Operator path cannot meet requirements. |
| `EliaAlberti/jev-rules` [R32] | ENGINEERING ONLY: task-relevant optional documentation selection. | Never allow the classifier to omit security, retention, approval, signing or release rules. Preserve existing hooks/config. |
| `kitze/skillbox` [R33] | QUALIFY only if existing skill governance cannot meet versioning/scoped access. | Avoid a second competing skill registry. Review hooks, execution permissions and data egress before any integration. |
| `itsmostafa/typesafe-mcp` [R34] | ENGINEERING ONLY: typed decision tooling when needed. | Its setup/key injection cannot bypass Operator for product calls. No `curl | sh`, unpinned installers or global config overwrite. |
| `mrnugget/jev-shell-history` [R38] | DO NOT ADOPT in the production application. | Shell history can contain secrets and proprietary commands; per-keystroke history egress is not needed for Métis. Any developer experiment is isolated, opt-in and sanitized. |
| `mysticalsin/laya` [R22] | QUALIFY COMPONENT: alternate served decision backend. | Do not conflate source license with zero compute cost or assume Jev-equivalent quality/context/calibration. |
| `mysticalsin/code-review-graph` [R35] | QUALIFY COMPONENT: canonical developer structural index if benchmarks support it. | Worktree/commit freshness and source validation mandatory; never embed its indexer in the user app. |
| `mysticalsin/graphify` [R36, R53] | QUALIFY narrow projection adapters for governed knowledge and, separately, optional developer architecture relationships. | Never a second authority store; separate domains, source/ACL validation and no unapproved content upload. |
| `DietrichGebert/ponytail` [R37] | ENGINEERING ONLY: reuse-first minimum-necessary-code discipline. | Complement the supplied refactoring skill. Validation, tests, accessibility and clear error handling are not “extra lines” to remove. |
| `vinzdg/codenotch` [R75] | ADAPT PATTERN: edge-attached rest, inward expansion, hysteresis/pinning, per-display positioning and recovery. | Inspect the actual selected implementation/license. Do not import another app, identity, credential readers, provider polling, remote listeners or updater. |

For copied/adapted code, record upstream repository/commit, exact files, applicable licenses and notices, local modifications, owner, security review and update strategy. A reference that fails qualification can still inform a small independently implemented pattern, but cannot be silently called production-ready. Do not add all projects merely because they are listed.

## 20. Verification strategy and release gates

### 20.1 Stable product requirement register

These IDs provide the minimum traceability spine. Link detailed section requirements, tasks, use cases, code changes and evidence to them; add finer IDs as necessary without renumbering established ones.

| M2-GOV-02 | Content, control artifacts and metadata kept separate | Approved M365 knowledge location; no private run content in Cloudflare storage/logs or template loophole. |
| M2-E2E-02 | Meeting-to-knowledge-to-Dust-to-skill full-chain proof | Actual meeting/Cloudflare/knowledge/Dust mutation/skill/version/Operator receipt under differing permissions. |



### 20.2 Test at the level of the claim

Use pure tests for parsers, geometry, state machines, accounting and policy. Use component/integration tests for real input, captions, callbacks and provider event semantics. Use native packaged tests for actual capture, windowing, automation, install/upgrade and signing. Use a permitted deployed environment for the portal/gateway chain. Keep fixtures, mocked services and real integrations labelled distinctly.

A source-regex contract is useful against accidental removal but is not a behavioral test. A screenshot is not proof of a click's effect. A model's DONE is not verification. A package-job pass is not proof of publisher signing or native user acceptance. A source SHA in workflow metadata may identify a PR head rather than the exact merge checkout; read the built application's provenance.

Use isolated synthetic accounts, notes, pages and files for destructive/side-effect tests. Never test by deleting the user's real files or sending real client messages. Keep sensitive data out of public CI, artifacts and support screenshots. Explicitly test that code/doc/page prompt injection cannot alter task authority.

### 20.3 Essential adversarial and reliability matrix

Include denied/expired authentication, revoked device, managed-policy precedence, consent revocation, forged renderer PCM/text, stale capture generations, delayed provider results, replay/echo, partial-speech negation, duplicate commands, multi-step failure, cancellation races, approval target changes, unsaved work, moved/covered browser targets, unsupported apps, URL/path injection, quotes/newlines/shell metacharacters, link-local/cloud metadata destinations, redirects, package tampering, signature mismatch, rollback/downgrade, low disk, interrupted downloads, captive portals, clock drift and D1 migration incompatibility.

For text passed to OS automation, use typed/native argument mechanisms and rigorous validation. Do not strip quotes from the user's title and declare the changed title correct; do not interpolate arbitrary text into shell/AppleScript source. Verify exact Unicode text through safe parameterization and target-specific constraints.

Test native Windows on the supported architecture and representative managed/unmanaged environments. Add ARM64 only when explicitly supported and qualified; do not advertise it from an architecture flag alone. Native Mac support follows its SDK/hardware capability matrix, with older supported OS/device behavior explicit. A shared interface does not establish identical underlying permission capabilities.

### 20.4 Windows signing and customer release

Use the legitimate configured publisher identity and existing qualified signing service/certificate. Sign the core executable, installer, required executable helpers and any relevant uninstaller/update payloads. Apply a trusted timestamp and strong digest policy; verify the actual downloaded artifacts with the appropriate Authenticode policy, expected publisher and chain, then compute final hashes. Never modify bytes after signing. Microsoft documents both code-signing choices and verification tooling. [R14, R39]

Signing is not a guarantee that every new binary will have immediate SmartScreen reputation. Do not disable SmartScreen, antivirus, revocation checking or enterprise policy to “fix” warnings. Document legitimate deployment/allowlisting and the actual signature result separately from reputation behavior. [R14]

Define one release evidence record containing source/checkout SHA, build-tool versions, core profile, platform/architecture, dependency locks, component-manifest revision, actual signer/timestamp, artifact hashes, test runs, schema/protocol compatibility and known limitations. Publish only the tested immutable artifact, not a later rebuild with the same filename. If bytes change, qualify the replacement.

### 20.4.1 The exact order of signing and acceptance

**Build → sign/timestamp the relevant Windows runtime/helpers/installer → verify signatures → compute final hashes → freeze candidate → run native acceptance against those bytes → approve → publish without rebuild → download and verify those same hashes.** Signing or timestamping after the final functional test changes the artifact and requires the applicable replacement-artifact qualification. TASK-063 creates the signed immutable Windows candidate; TASK-064 verifies and promotes it. Investigate unsigned debug builds earlier, but never label their passes final signed-customer evidence. [R39]

Inventory signing prerequisites during TASK-002, not on release day: publisher identity, permitted signing service/certificate, secret access, timestamp endpoint, signing of packaged helpers and the actual update/uninstaller route. Public signing must not use developer/self-signed credentials. Treat missing required timestamps or warnings as explicit evidence gaps even if a tool invocation otherwise exits successfully. Verify expected publisher/chain/revocation policy and post-upload bytes; a matching checksum alone does not establish trusted origin.

### 20.4.2 Enterprise deployment and service operational evidence

Test the chosen supported distribution in normal non-admin user conditions and through the organization's actual managed deployment route. Document unattended install/upgrade behavior where supported, application detection, exit codes, restart requirements, proxy/PAC and trusted-certificate conditions, offline core installation, controlled model downloads and clean uninstall. Do not disable TLS validation or endpoint protection to make an installation succeed. Do not promise MSI/MSIX/ARM64/MDM compatibility from an untested packaging flag; qualify each advertised route without imposing an unnecessary new installer format.

Freeze the service protocol/schema/policy/component versions compatible with each client candidate. Deploy additive compatible backend changes before dependent clients, qualify an internal canary and retain rollback compatibility. Test expired credentials, provider overload, database/knowledge outage, network partitions, dropped metering events, stale policy, service restart and failed release promotion. Define ownership, escalation, measured recovery objectives and an actual restore exercise. A content-free Operator backup is different from authorized knowledge backup; both must restore only their approved data classes and preserve deletion restrictions.

### 20.5 Independent platform gates

The earlier workflow required tags on current main and coupled platform release requirements. Implement an explicit reviewed release-policy change for Métis 2.0: platform-qualified Windows assets can publish once all required Windows and shared gates pass, while the Mac native public lane remains on hold. Preserve the intended main/review integration rule unless an approved repository policy explicitly changes it. No force-push, no bypassed branch protection and no global `continue-on-error` for release safety.

| Gate | Windows public 2.0 | Native Mac QA | Native Mac public |
|---|---|---|---|
| Shared schemas/security/data policy | Required | Required | Required |
| Platform-specific functional/UX/capture tests | Required Windows evidence | Required Mac evidence | Same qualified Mac artifact lineage |
| Transcript/provider qualification for enabled profiles | Required | Required | Required |
| Proper customer signing | Required | Development-policy signing only if permitted | Required Apple distribution signing |
| Apple notarization / App Store qualification as applicable | Not applicable | Not public assurance | Required for the actual distribution route |
| PCC eligibility | Not a Windows capability | Only legitimate eligible testing | Required only if advertising PCC, under authorized distribution |
| Publication status | Publish when all applicable gates pass | Clearly labelled, controlled QA | BLOCKED until missing Apple inputs and all other gates clear |

Do not advance a Mac update feed to a nonexistent native DMG or promote unnotarized QA bytes through a stable channel. Keep native Mac and legacy Electron Mac transition/version policy explicit. Preserve the Windows stable feed and previous Mac availability without pretending both products were released together.

### 20.6 Scope-aware gates without silently shrinking the product

Track evidence by **requirement × use case × platform × profile × artifact/service revision**, not one checkbox per feature. A Windows pass does not qualify native Mac; a development Mac build does not qualify public notarization; an approved post-meeting import does not qualify raw-media automatic attendance. Keep the whole 2.0 commitment visible while allowing independent implementation and explicitly approved bounded releases.

For a Windows public release, require all shared controls and all Windows-advertised capabilities, including their actual services. Pending Apple public signing is not a dependency. A genuinely unavailable external platform program may remain BLOCKED and disabled with owner-approved release notes; it is never counted as a completed feature. A missing Jev/Laya service or disabled core action that the release promises is not a convenient platform exception. Do not invent a passing aggregate because every difficult row was marked unsupported.

## 21. Numbered execution plan — TASK-001 to TASK-066

### 21.1 Execution contract and order

This is the single revision-4 execution plan using the **same TASK-001–TASK-066 identities as revision 3**. Start with the numbered checkpoints in section 0, then select the lowest-numbered ready task in each approved work lane. TASK-027.A and TASK-028.A are the named early P1/input repair exception, not deferred until all optional models work. Section 21.3 preserves revision-2 lineage. All 55 requirements and UC-001–UC-112 retain their explicit scope; section 25 proves document coverage without claiming product completion.

Each task has a versioned tracked unit: IDs, owner, branch/SHA, dependencies, scoped plan, changed files, commands/exits, environment, actual evidence, rollback, status and next operation. Use NOT STARTED, IN PROGRESS, IMPLEMENTED, VERIFIED, BLOCKED or DEFERRED. IMPLEMENTED is not deployed or verified. Only evidence at the level claimed closes the corresponding gate. Deferral of a required capability needs owner approval; no silent omission to achieve a green release.

The numbered order is for dependency management, not forced idling. Independent work may run in parallel using disjoint ownership/worktrees and bounded interface contracts; live integration remains blocked until prerequisites pass. Native Mac engineering is required, but unavailable Apple public signing/entitlement must not block a separately qualified Windows release. Missing tenant/media/privacy approval is an explicit blocker for that capability, not permission to bypass it or portray a manual fallback as full automatic participation.

A production mutation, public release, new paid service or broader account permission still follows actual authorization. Use synthetic non-sensitive fixtures in route bring-up. No admin consent, recording permission, contract approval, test result or deployment ID may be invented.

| Phase | Tasks | Focus | Checkpoint |
|---|---|---|---|
| A | TASK-001–010 | Baseline, identity, authority and product contracts | Current source/policies, knowledge boundaries, simple Settings, skills and Teams eligibility. |
| B | TASK-011–020 | Private real Cloudflare speech | Configured privacy-safe server transport, actual capture/segments/wake and synthetic speech proof. |
| C | TASK-021–034 | Lean applications, native UX, decisions and ledger | Optional packs, signed core path, onboarding, orb, actions and authoritative usage. |
| D | TASK-035–045 | Shared Intelligence, Dust and central skills | Actual canonical writes/projections, authorized reads, Dust collaboration, server skills and portal. |
| E | TASK-046–052 | Visible meeting participation | Teams app/SSO, real lifecycle/admission, qualified media, source ownership and audience-safe output. |
| F | TASK-053–060 | Cross-system quality, privacy and efficiency | Transcription, knowledge/Dust/skills/meeting evidence, rights handling and measured resource budgets. |
| G | TASK-061–066 | Refactor, operations and platform release | Preserved behavior, deployed operations, immutable qualification, signed Windows and honest Mac hold. |

### 21.1.1 Prerequisite semantics for independent implementations

Suffixes such as `.WIN`, `.MAC`, `.CORE`, `.TEAMS`, `.A` and `.B` identify evidence slices under a stable task; they are not new root task IDs. A task can have a verified Windows slice and a blocked Mac slice, with aggregate status PARTIAL. Define `.CONTRACT`, `.ADAPTER` and `.INTEGRATION` outputs where an end-to-end test would otherwise depend circularly on the adapter it is testing. For example, TASK-033 can consume TASK-031.ADAPTER while the combined applied-command proof closes in TASK-056; TASK-030 consumes TASK-028.A, and TASK-028.B checks the completed surface rather than gating its construction. `Depends on` lists common implementation prerequisites; the task's explicit integration requirements name additional providers/platforms needed to close a particular slice. The generated task graph must represent both and reject cycles or missing references.

Shared schemas and synthetic contract fixtures allow independent development; they never count as live integration evidence. Do not require TASK-029 native Mac completion to implement Windows UI/actions, TASK-031 Jev account access to bring up Laya, or TASK-055 full Teams participation to test a desktop command. Conversely, closing those integration claims still requires the appropriate real provider/platform run.

Run initial authorization/privacy/quality tests alongside each change, not only during the final numbered acceptance sweep. Native Mac engineering and speech benchmarks start as soon as their actual prerequisites exist. Final integration/refactor gates revalidate the completed scope and do not supersede earlier security checks.

### 21.1.2 Explicit slice handoffs: what may start and what may close

These handoffs are the required decomposition where a root task spans independent platforms or a later integrated result. Other tasks may keep one slice. Create these names in the execution registry before running its scoped-DAG validator. Referencing `.ADAPTER` requires that adapter's actual contract/service proof, not its parent task's later UI or business demonstration.

| Slice | Prerequisite outputs | Closure / consumer |
|---|---|---|
| TASK-027.A | TASK-001 baseline; TASK-002 scope; TASK-005 shared contract | Narrow #196/#197 implementation and regression proof; no optional model prerequisite. |
| TASK-027.B | TASK-027.A; TASK-020; TASK-022; TASK-024; TASK-026 | Final fresh/upgrade onboarding with real cloud default and selected-component behavior. |
| TASK-028.A | TASK-001 baseline; TASK-002; TASK-005 | Usable existing right-edge geometry/input; supplies TASK-030, not blocked by it. |
| TASK-030.WIN | TASK-019; TASK-028.A; shared UI/session contract | Actual React orb/beam/voice and caption integration. TASK-030.MAC additionally consumes TASK-029 native readiness. |
| TASK-028.B | TASK-028.A; TASK-027.B; same-platform TASK-030 | Requalification of right-edge interactions with the completed command surface; does not gate construction of TASK-030. |
| TASK-031.ADAPTER | TASK-005; TASK-012; TASK-015 | Real entitled Jev service and applied candidate contract with synthetic safe state; consumed by actions and Intelligence. |
| TASK-032.ADAPTER | TASK-005; TASK-012; TASK-015 | Real qualified Laya service without a Jev credential dependency; consumed by actions and Intelligence. |
| TASK-033.WIN | TASK-005; TASK-019; actual adapter for each model-assisted variant | Exact native action and verification; .MAC additionally consumes TASK-029. Deterministic tests need no paid model. |
| TASK-034.CORE | TASK-005; TASK-015 | Identity/ledger invariants and schema; subsequent route slices add real provider reconciliation, not a new ledger. |
| TASK-045.CORE | TASK-034.CORE plus its deployed read path | Actual full-window people/usage/error views; later panes depend on their own real knowledge, skill and provider integrations. |
| TASK-040 assessments | TASK-037 evidence; TASK-031.ADAPTER and/or TASK-032.ADAPTER for each variant | Facts/assessments/explanations verified separately from commands; both advertised providers need actual evidence. |
| TASK-056 platform slice | TASK-027.B; same-platform TASK-028.B/TASK-030/TASK-033; TASK-034.CORE; TASK-045.CORE; TASK-053; TASK-054 | Actual desktop speech/action/portal journey. No TASK-055 prerequisite; Mac and Windows results independent. |
| TASK-055 live Teams slice | TASK-050 live source plus the root task's knowledge/skill prerequisites | Actual meeting-to-Dust/skill proof; an imported transcript cannot close this slice. |
| TASK-057.CORE | Its root task's core privacy/control evidence plus TASK-056 core results | Core no-content-persistence proof. TASK-057.TEAMS additionally consumes live-media evidence and does not gate unrelated development. |
| TASK-063.WIN | All relevant Windows/shared final evidence, TASK-061 final sweep, TASK-062 operations and required advertised-service slices | Sign/freeze/test the candidate. Apple public-signing state is excluded; missing required Windows/shared controls are not. |
| TASK-064 | TASK-063.WIN final signed-artifact receipt and release approval | Promote exact bytes and verify the actual distribution/update feed. |

TASK-031/TASK-032 applied-command and Intelligence integration receipts are produced by TASK-056 and TASK-040 respectively; those receipts close the parent feature promise after consuming the already qualified `.ADAPTER`. This is a directed handoff, not a circular prerequisite in which an adapter must first wait for its consumer. Keep each parent's aggregate state PARTIAL until all its required consumers are proven.

## 22. Required evidence and final handoff

### 22.1 Completion states

Use PLANNED, IMPLEMENTED, VERIFIED, BLOCKED and DEFERRED with evidence at the level claimed. VERIFIED means an identified test/environment/artifact demonstrates the requirement. A manual statement, branch title, mock screenshot, model assessment or planned script is not sufficient. DEFERRED needs the owner's explicit scope decision; do not quietly defer a required feature because it is difficult.

Each completed milestone records requirement and use-case IDs, changed files, commit/PR, test commands and exits, environment, platform/profile, artifact or trace references, known limitations and rollback. A blocked milestone records the exact missing permission/input/service and what independent work continues. Do not report an unexplained “98% complete” or a numeric enterprise-readiness score as assurance.

### 22.2 Minimum delivery evidence

Deliver a repository PRD/architecture decision update; threat and data-flow matrices; shared capability schemas; all requested reference dispositions; transcript benchmark report; before/after package/runtime metrics; native and React state captures; actual Jev and Laya command demonstrations; portal reconciliation report; install/component security results; native Mac eligibility/signing status; signed Windows artifact provenance; and a concise user/IT operating guide.

The visual demonstration must include the actual command utterance, live caption above the bar, solving orb inside it, real target effect and verification. Use an uncut run and real latency boundaries. For the audit, show the operation's route/model/attempt and usage metadata separately. Never stage a successful response over a prerecorded action and label it live.

Also deliver the Cloudflare route/privacy evidence record and sanitized configuration readback, no-content-sentinel results, device-qualified local-model catalog, Settings lifecycle tests, and requested-track speech accounting proof. These are required even when the visual and decision demonstrations already work.

Also deliver the canonical authority/storage matrix, read/write/projection schemas, plaintext-wiki sharing review, actual Dust personal/service connection evidence, conflicting-write/deletion/ACL test results, skill version promotion/revocation demonstration, Settings inventory/task-study results, Teams app registrations/permission matrix, real join/media/status evidence and the deployment-specific privacy-owner decision record. Unsupported other-platform routes remain explicitly labelled and disabled.

For installation, publish a waterfall separating bootstrap bytes, core bytes, optional packs, verification, extraction, sign-in wait and first usable action. Record remaining disk usage and temporary peak. A smaller bootstrap alone does not satisfy the lightweight requirement.

### 22.3 Final Codex report format

Report: (1) what actually changed; (2) which required journeys passed on which immutable artifacts; (3) transcript quality by supported slice and remaining limitations; (4) package/memory/latency before and after; (5) actual portal reconciliation and backend states; (6) exact Windows signer/timestamp/hash and release location if published; (7) native Mac implemented/tested versus signing/PCC/distribution blocked; and (8) the smallest next steps for remaining blockers.

Finish with a reusable continuation record containing current branch/SHA, dirty-work ownership, active tasks, decisions, evidence and next commands. Do not promise unstarted background work. Do not claim the installer, portal or provider was deployed unless the actual authorized operation succeeded.

### 22.4 Paste-in launch instruction

```text
Implement the attached Métis 2.0 revision 4 complete delivery contract.
Do not stop at analysis, a roadmap, a mockup or dependency installation.

Read section 0 and the current repository instructions. Start at TASK-001.
Preserve all 66 stable task IDs, all 55 requirement IDs and UC-001–UC-112.
Use sections 25–27 for owner coverage, golden journeys and evidence checks.

Run the immediate TASK-027.A / TASK-028.A P1/input repair lane after baseline
and contracts. Then follow the ready-task dependencies. Split platform and
provider evidence: Windows need not wait for Apple public signing, Laya need
not wait for a Jev key, and core commands need not wait for Teams media.
Never count those still-required integrations as done merely because another
slice passes. Only the owner can approve a narrower advertised release.

Implement the real Cloudflare-first speech path with server credentials and
qualified no-content-persistence controls. Keep optional local speech and
local generation separate, device-qualified and explicitly installed/selected.
Deliver the actual solving orb inside the beam bar with live captions above,
one trusted microphone owner, graceful actions and verified outcomes.

Make Jev/Laya useful in both desktop actions and evidence-based Intelligence.
Complete canonical knowledge, scoped wiki/graph, real Dust read/write,
central skill versioning/execution, accurate Operator accounting, simpler
Settings, Entra and visible eligible Teams participation. Preserve every
privacy, source/audience, permission, platform and release boundary.

Use characterization-backed refactoring and a fresh bounded source map.
Prove the exact video journey, the native Windows/Mac results, the shared-key
fleet path, full meeting/knowledge/Dust/skill flow, privacy and recovery.

Build -> sign/timestamp -> freeze hashes -> test the exact signed candidate
-> publish those same bytes. Do not rebuild after final acceptance.
Keep genuine native Mac engineering active and Apple public distribution
explicitly gated. Report proposed, implemented, deployed, verified and blocked
states separately, with exact source/artifact/service evidence and next steps.
```
